At a ~40 px body the silhouette is barely legible and COLOUR is what says *this is theirs, not yours*, which is the real defect in borrowing: a claw that reads as player gear reads as **loot you could pick up**. **Wiring them up found three things and only the first was expected.** `rotationOffsetRad` had to be re-measured, as the prompt file predicted — the old ~-161°/+174° were cancelling the SPEAR's and HAMMER's baked pointing direction, a property of those files; the new -47.1°/-53.8° are not near-zero because "socket upper-left, business end lower-right" produces a genuinely DIAGONAL composition where `gun_default` runs closer to horizontal. **`scale` could not be inherited either, and nothing would have caught that**: the diagonal puts a ~200 px-long object in a ~160 px-wide texture, and `rigComposition.test.ts`'s module-proportion band measures WIDTH — so keeping the placeholder's divisor would have passed every gate while rendering these ~40% longer than what they replace; the divisors are chosen against along-axis length instead (55.7 vs 55.6 authoring px, 65.6 vs 65.5). **And measuring the ANCHOR is worth ten lines**, which every previous batch eyeballed: taking the centroid of the alpha mass in the first 12% of the long axis put both new entries at **0.0°** tip error driven live against a real target, where the shipped `enemygun` measured the same way in the same frame is **18.2°** off — the standing cost of the eyeballed-anchor convention, never before quantified. Two verification lessons that generalize: `worldTransform` is stale outside a render pass and hands back the UNTRANSFORMED texture angle (the first measurement returned exactly that, and the only tell was the reference weapon also reading "correct"), and always put a SHIPPED weapon in the frame as the control, since a harness reporting 0° for everything is measuring nothing. Also: the generator returned WebP, which nothing on the Node side of this repo decodes (Pillow converts it losslessly, verified pixel-for-pixel), and both files came back with **zero pixels at alpha 255** — the 250-254 plateau + 1-10 veil `alphaClamp.mjs` exists for. `art` `render` `docs`
- **09-06** [The game gets its name: Blightbloom /《绽晶》](roadmap/39-2026-09-06-energy-card-capacity.md#the-game-gets-its-name-blightbloom-2026-09-06-docs--client--server-no-engine-change) — no engine change; the last open item in design/13 closes. *"DayDayUp"* was a codename and had to go for a reason unrelated to taste: **天天向上 is Hunan TV's flagship variety show**, the worst collision to carry into a WeChat mini-game name review, and the name is an unownable Chinglish meme besides. `Blightbloom` is design/13's own two poles in one word — the Blight that crystallises the world and the bloom the enemies literally are; 《绽晶》 is not a translation but a second name on the same image (绽 burst-into-bloom + 晶 crystal), leading with the bloom where the English leads with the rot. **The rejections are the useful part.** 《枯潮》, the first choice, died on trademark — two characters ending in the *identical* 潮 as **《鸣潮》** (Wuthering Waves, mark registered 2021-07) in the same classes, and CN defensive-registration practice explicitly covers *"在相同类别注册与其商标音同、形似等的相似商标"*. 《枯晶》 replaced it, was legally clean, and died on **discoverability**: search engines rewrite 枯晶 to **《晶核》** (ByteDance-published), a failure mode no trademark search surfaces — found only because the check was run as a real search and the engine did it to us mid-query. `blightbloom.com`/`.net` are unregistered, verified by RDAP against a **working control**, which is the only thing that makes a 404 evidence rather than a dead endpoint. **The code line: a name a human READS moves, an identifier a machine MATCHES does not** — that decided ~30 occurrences without a per-file judgement. Moved: `mainMenu.title` in all eight locales (and 绽晶 for `zh`, the first Chinese title this game has ever had — `zh.ts` had carried the English `'DAYDAYUP'` its whole life), `<title>`, Capacitor `appName`, the WeChat project name, the replay-rejection throw, the boot-failure prefix, every `[blightbloom]` server log line. Held, each now commented so a later reader does not "finish the job": the `daydayup.*` localStorage keys (renaming wipes every save and login), `REPLAY_FILE_KIND` (matched against bytes on disk — renaming rejects every replay already saved), `daydayup.db` + the `/health` `service:` ids (matched from outside this repo), the `daydayup-*` Worker names (a renamed Worker is a NEW Worker with the domain still bound to the old one) and `de.elk.daydayup` (a changed bundle id is a new app). `usernameFilter` gained `blightbloom` and **kept** `daydayup`. The title got a test pinning en/zh and — the actual invariant — that every other locale keeps the Latin title, since `Translations<typeof en>` checks that a key exists and never that its value is still the brand. Chinese half is **暂定** until CNIPA classes 9+41 and WeChat's 名称唯一性 clear; 枯潮 survives as the in-fiction name of the Blight. `docs` `i18n` `platform` `test`
- **09-06** [The BGM gets quieter and slower, and the tempo turns out to live in the file](roadmap/39-2026-09-06-energy-card-capacity.md#the-bgm-gets-quieter-and-slower-and-the-tempo-turns-out-to-live-in-the-file-2026-09-06-client--tools--docs-no-engine-change) — a balance request in plain language (*"背景音乐的音量大概是音效的一半，节奏是目前的0.7倍"*) splits into one one-line fix and one real pipeline problem, because only one has a runtime knob. **Volume**: new-install `music` default moved from 0.5 to 0.25 (half of `sfx`'s 0.5); a returning player's own choice is untouched. **Tempo has no runtime knob at all — every track is a fixed AI-generated master** — and the first attempt (`HTMLMediaElement.playbackRate` / `InnerAudioContext.playbackRate` at 0.7) was reverted once shipped: WeChat's `InnerAudioContext` documents no pitch-preservation for `playbackRate` where web's `preservesPitch` is a real guarantee, so the identical multiplier would ship a DIFFERENT pitch per platform. Baked into the file instead — `process_music.py`'s new `TEMPO_FACTOR` via `pip install pedalboard`'s `time_stretch` (Rubber Band, pitch preserved) — which took two more wrong turns to actually pass the gate: stretching the already-cut region in isolation blew the loop seam's band-diff from ~1.5 dB to 6.6 dB (`menu`) / 2.2-2.9 dB (`boss`), because the algorithm had no musical context beyond the clip's own edges; stretching the WHOLE master first fixed `boss` but not `menu`, which falsified "isolated clip is the whole problem" — the algorithm is content-adaptive, not a uniform transform, so a region tuned NATIVELY can fail to close once stretched regardless of context. The actual fix was this pipeline's own recurring lesson (the search-vs-gate drift table in `tools/audio-pipeline/README.md`, now four rows deep) applied again: `search_regions` had to rank candidates on the STRETCHED signal, because that is what ships. New regions for both tracks — `menu` 68.0 s (was 69.0), `boss` 47.5 s (was 64.5) — both comfortably inside the 2.5 dB gate (1.77 dB / 1.60 dB), and the music subpackage dropped 1.09 MB → 0.84 MB as a side effect of the shorter loops. `audit.py --class music` passes both files; `npm run check` green across all eight workspace packages. **Still open**: nobody has listened to either loop at the new tempo — the gates confirm the seam and mix level, not whether 0.7x reads as relaxed rather than sluggish. `audio` `tools` `docs`


**[2026-09-07 — the backend goes live, and then gets tested](roadmap/40-2026-09-07-deploy-tests.md)**

- **09-07** [The three planes go live on a borrowed box](roadmap/40-2026-09-07-deploy-tests.md#the-three-planes-go-live-on-a-borrowed-box-2026-09-0607-server--ci--docs-no-engine-change) — *recorded retroactively on 2026-09-07; that pass shipped, moved ROADMAP 9.0 to 🟢 and updated design/19 + `server/deploy/README.md`, but never wrote its log entry.* ROADMAP 9.0, found 2026-09-05 while writing Phase 9's setup steps: a Paddle webhook is a server-to-server POST to a public HTTPS URL and this project had no server deployment of any kind, which outranked the credential question because no credential can be exercised without an address. `gameserver`/`matchsvc`/`billsvc` now run as three containers from one image (`server/Dockerfile` + `server/docker-compose.yml`, process picked by `command:`) on the same company VPS and Cloudflare zone the sibling project's backend uses, behind the same Caddy instance, at `bb.gamestao.com`; the client points at it by default. **The build step is the part the design section had not anticipated**: this server imports live TypeScript from sibling workspaces through path aliases (`@dd/engine`, `@dd/game/*`, `@dd/net/*`), so rsync-and-run would have meant shipping the whole monorepo and running `npm ci` on the VPS — `server/scripts/build.mjs` esbuild-bundles each entrypoint into one flat ESM file instead, `ws` and `node:sqlite` external, satisfied by a nine-line `server/deploy/package.json`. CI deploys through a key pinned to a **forced command**, whose live copy sits OUTSIDE the deploy target (installed inside it, a deploy could replace the script and the constraint would be gone) and which moves five things, never `docker-compose.yml` or `.env` — that is what keeps the key unable to write a compose file bind-mounting the host's `/` into a container. *(Correction, 2026-09-07 (volume 42): that last clause was false when written — the script has always copied `docker-compose.yml`, and the manifest test asserts it is in the payload. Only `.env` is untouched. The capability is real and now documented as such in `deploy/ci-deploy.sh`; recorded here rather than silently edited, because a work-log entry is history and this one's security claim is the kind a reader would otherwise rely on.)* Success is each container answering its own `/health`, not the command returning 0. Everything on the box is named `wnet-test`, not after this game: borrowed idle capacity, torn down whole with one `rm -rf`. **billsvc stays in dev-stub mode** — no Paddle credential exists, and `startupGuard.ts` refuses that flag under `NODE_ENV=production` outright. `net` `platform` `docs`
- **09-07** [The deploy layer gets tested](roadmap/40-2026-09-07-deploy-tests.md#the-deploy-layer-gets-tested-2026-09-07-server-tests--build-script-no-engine-change) — asked in one line (*"服务器部分有测试可以加吗"*), and the answer is where it went. `server/src` measured **99.56% lines / 97.93% branches**, and the 27 uncovered branches read one by one are almost all defensive arms a nearer guard already shadows — testing those means poking private state and pins nothing. **The gap was the pass above.** Five files (`scripts/build.mjs`, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `deploy/package.json`, `deploy/ci-deploy.sh`), none imported by anything under `src/`, none reachable from a test, all outside `coverage.include` — so no percentage could ever have moved to say so, and the artifact that runs in production is not the code the suite tests. Now design/18's **Layer 6**. `deploy.bundle.test.ts` builds into a scratch dir in the OS temp tree and links in ONLY what `deploy/package.json` declares (standing in for `npm install --omit=dev`); the upward `node_modules` walk from there finds nothing of this monorepo, so a bundle reaching for an undeclared module fails exactly as it would in the container — proven by deleting `ws`, which reds two of the three boots with the production stack trace as the failure message. Each bundle boots as a bare `node` process and must answer `/health` with its **own** service name, since `{ok: true}` alone would wave through a mis-mapped `entries` row. `deploy.manifests.test.ts` cross-checks bundle names across build script / compose `command:` / the forced command's payload check / CI's `tar` list, the externals against `deploy/package.json` (exact versions only — a range means the deployed bytes can change without a commit), the base image's Node major against `build.mjs`'s `target`, every compose env var against what `src/` actually reads, that no secret is inlined where `env_file: .env` is the mechanism, that each service healthchecks **its own** port, and that every internal `http://` names a real service at that service's port. The two a percentage could never reach: a **renamed env var** leaves compose passing a value nobody reads while the process runs on its default — the exact failure design/19 §9 recorded from the sibling project and left as a thing to design against, in a sentence that ended *"this project has no deploy mechanism yet"* and now points here — and a **copy-pasted service block** leaves a healthcheck polling its neighbour's port, reporting a dead container healthy. `build.mjs` was refactored to EXPORT `entries`/`external`/`target` + `buildAll(outdir)` behind the same run-directly guard `index.ts` uses, so the tests read real values instead of a second copy. The hand-rolled compose reader is guarded by a first `describe` that fails if a reformat makes it match nothing, because passing over an empty set is that pattern's failure mode. billsvc's compose env is fed to the real `assertBillingStartupSafety` **with a control** (the same env at `NODE_ENV=production` must throw), without which the first case passes against a guard that never throws. Four `src` branch cases were worth adding and three were not: a disconnect BEFORE launch, a `resume` into a room that never lost anyone (asserted through the frame counter — a doubled metronome is a match at double speed), an integrity kick against a seat that already dropped, a kick taking the room's last connection, plus a bot torn down before its match starts; four mutants killed. The rest are commented in the source as unreachable, and one correction fell out of the mutation run — `BotClient`'s null-timer guard is NOT load-bearing (Node's `clearInterval` tolerates a null handle), so the test says it pins the teardown rather than a TypeError. Suite 1004 → **1031** across 48 files, `src` branches 97.93% → **98.31%**. **Still open**: nothing here runs Docker, and a variable that reaches the VPS's untracked `.env` but not the process is still invisible to anything tracked. `net` `test` `docs`


**[2026-09-07 — a game-portal build target](roadmap/41-2026-09-07-crazygames.md)**

- **09-07** [The CrazyGames build target](roadmap/41-2026-09-07-crazygames.md#the-crazygames-build-target-2026-09-07-client--build--docs-no-engine-change) — asked as a question (*“距离发布 crazy games，还缺少什么？整个游戏流程贯通了吗”*) and then as an instruction to close every gap in order. **The flow was already whole**, and it was verified rather than believed: menu → SELECT MODE → SOLO PvE → Forge → START RUN → a live room, `FLOOR 1/5` `ROOM 1/5` `FOES 8`, zero console errors and zero network requests (a solo run needs no backend). The content behind it is thin, not broken — one biome, five authored floors, one 60-room arena, the tutorial — and Backlog B1/B2 stay the largest holes in the loop as *designed*. **What was missing was a platform layer: `crazygames` appeared ZERO times in the tree.** Eleven gaps closed. New `client/src/platform/crazygames/`: a `sdk.ts` façade where every call no-ops without the script and every wait is bounded (an ad network may not be able to end a run), `AdController` (never during gameplay, never during an online match, muted + `ticker.stop()` for the duration, released in a `finally`), `BannerHost` (one 320×50 container, main menu only, hidden AND cleared elsewhere, 30 s refresh floor), `GameplayBracket`, `PortalSession`. Relative paths in **two** halves that do not cover each other — `base: './'` for what Vite writes, `baseAssetHost` for the ~200 absolute `'/skins/…'` paths in our own source, producing `skins/x.png` and NOT `./skins/x.png` because `webMusicDeck` tests `el.src.endsWith(url)` and `el.src` reads back absolute, so a `./` prefix would restart a multi-megabyte download on every loop wrap. The Forge's STORE entry is not rendered and `[B]` not bound on a portal host (in-game purchases there are invite-only through the platform's own Xsolla account) — `storePlatform`'s fail-closed shape, but the FIRST host it cannot feature-detect: a portal page has a `document`, a `fetch` and no `wx`, so `platform/hostKind.ts` is a DECLARED fact. Four clicks to gameplay became one (`MainMenu.setQuickPlay` + `RunLifecycle.beginQuickRun`, which art-gates for itself) with SELECT MODE on its own button, so nothing became unreachable. **Touch controls were invisible until the first touch** — `TouchVisual.active` was `everTouched`, so a phone player saw no joystick, no fire button and no weapon buttons until they had already guessed where to press; `setAssumeTouch` is declared by the platform from a `(pointer: coarse)` probe (primary pointer, so a touchscreen laptop still gets no overlay). Plus `lang="zh-CN"` → `en`, a title still reading *“Vertical Slice”*, the `user-select`/`touch-action` suppression the docs ask for by name, arrow/space scroll cancelled on every repeat of a held key and yielding to a focused text field, a factual data notice on `LoginScreen` in all eight locales, and `audio/externalMute.ts` — a module sink (the third instance of `uiSound`/`musicDirector`'s shape, same reason: a 500-line-capped `Game.ts` between the muter and the one authority) that is a FACTOR over the settings, never a write, so an ad restores 25% to 25% and leaves a self-muted player muted. **The design decision worth defending: the game does not know the portal exists.** `PortalSession` derives all of it from ONE input — the phase, once per frame, on its own ticker callback installed by the entry point; nothing under `src/game/` imports it and `Game.ts`/`GameLoop.ts` gained nothing. Five hooks whose correctness is “nobody forgot one” is the shape `musicDirector` already records as having two failure modes. Two phases are not what they look like, both caught by tests: `settings` is a full phase that behaves like an overlay (so `paused → settings` read as “left a run” and put an ad over a run the player was returning to), and `start()` resolves AFTER the first frame (so the session's only `menu` transition went past with the SDK gate still closed and the banner never appeared at all). **Three findings the SDK docs do not contain**, all from serving the real bundle from a sub-path and driving it, all the same class — the shipped SDK is not the documented SDK, which is the risk `sdk.ts` was built to survive: `SDK.environment` **does not exist** in v2.9.0 (`'environment' in SDK` is `false`; the prototype has `getEnvironment()`), `getEnvironment()` returns a **Promise**, and `SDK.init()` **can never settle** — unbounded, that leaves `loadingStop()` uncalled and the portal spinning over a playable game. `__portal.diagnostics()` exists for exactly this and is the only instrument this repo has for the half it cannot test. **Online play verified against the deployed backend** (*“我希望能直接测试联机功能”*): PVP SOLO QUEUE → the server's 30 s practice-bot backfill → a real match, `ALIVE 2/2`, two seats, lockstep advancing **897 ticks in four seconds**. Two live tabs could not do it — a background tab's `setTimeout` is throttled to ~1/min, so the second client's queue entry expired while a manual probe stayed `queued`. That test found a real bug: the portal build inherited `runState.ts`'s `http://localhost:8788`, right for `npm run dev` and the one default that can never be right for an upload, where co-op/PvP/ladder/accounts would fail to connect **silently** on a build that works perfectly locally. `portalBuild.test.ts` is design/18 Layer 6's technique applied to the client — it asserts against the real `index.html` and imports the real config to exercise its OWN transform — and caught that the HTML rewrite was on the wrong hook entirely (`vite:build-html` has already resolved the entry by `generateBundle`, so a `transformIndexHtml`-only plugin cannot swap it in a production build; the build failed on the plugin's own guard, which is the only reason it was not a silently mis-built bundle). Client suite 5203 → **5218**, repo **8664** green, coverage 97.52%/92.56% over **241** client files. Bundle 6.2 MB, initial download only the `lobby` pack + UI — a property to protect, since the platform grades from page open to the first `gameplayStart`. **Still open** (design/20): a registered domain, without which banner fill and real ad playback cannot be settled; ~~a rewarded-ad PLACEMENT~~ (**settled and shipped the same day** — volume 42; the reward is a doubled extraction payout, which is the only candidate that clears `05`'s wipe rule and `14`'s sell-breadth rule at once); a hosted privacy URL; the upload itself. `platform` `net` `ui` `docs` `test`
- **09-07** [The nine sentences that said "both entries"](roadmap/41-2026-09-07-crazygames.md#the-nine-sentences-that-said-both-entries-2026-09-07-docs-only-no-code-change) — the close-out audit for the pass above, and it found more than a status box. A third entry point is the same mechanism as a schema gaining one field: `grep -rn "both entries\\|two entry points" design/*.md` returned **nine hits in three docs**, and every one outside ROADMAP was FALSIFIED — `04` (2), `11` (4), `12` (3) — because a sentence counting a set has no "still true" reading once the count changes, which is how this differs from the `SkinDef`-gains-`maxEnergy` case that recorded the pattern. Rephrased to **"every entry"** rather than "all three" so a fourth target falsifies nothing; the exception is `11`'s source-guard bullet, a claim about that guard's own coverage, which names all three files and says the enumeration is three long. **The tests were already right and the prose was nine sentences behind** — the filename enumerations in `audioPipeline`/`musicPipeline`/`wechatPhasedBoot` were extended with the code, so every gate was green while the docs still described two entries: a green suite is not evidence the prose is current, and extending an enumeration in a test is the signal to grep its cardinality word. One unrelated falsehood fell out: `11` called `Game.ts` "the repo's one tracked 500-line offender", contradicted 190 lines later in the same doc by "the file-length baseline is empty". Also: `design/18`'s **Layer 6 was still described as server-only** while a client test had joined it the same day (table, title and scope paragraph now cover both sides), `design/00`'s platform matrix gained the portal row, and `.claude/launch.json` gained `client-crazygames-dev` — with a note that it serves from the ROOT and so cannot prove the one thing this target is likeliest to get wrong. `current-state.md` deliberately untouched: it tracks `ENGINE_VERSION` prose and this bumped nothing. `docs`

**[2026-09-07 — the rewarded ad, the backups, and a report that closed by not happening](roadmap/42-2026-09-07-rewarded-ad.md)**

- **09-07** [The rewarded ad is an OFFER, and the offer is the part that was missing](roadmap/42-2026-09-07-rewarded-ad.md#the-rewarded-ad-is-an-offer-and-the-offer-is-the-part-that-was-missing-2026-09-07-client--docs-no-engine-change) — *"即使我上免费版，也需要接入 crazy games 的广告"*. Volume 41 shipped the ad layer entire — transport, policy, adblock probe, all tested — and filed the rewarded ad as open because the reward is a balance decision. **What was missing was never the ad; it was the OFFER.** Decided by the owner from three candidates: **double the extraction payout**, the only one that clears both locked rules — `05`'s wipe rule kills every continue-shaped reward (so the offer is *absent* on the defeat screen, and the test asserts `undefined` rather than `null` so a later pass cannot wire one in and grey it out), and `14`'s "sell breadth, not power" picks the currency (a material is the farmable half of the economy, spendable only on blueprints already owned). **The non-ad player's guarantee is ORDERING, not a second code path**: the baseline bag is banked BEFORE the offer is drawn and the reward is the same bag banked a second time, so ignoring the button keeps 100% — asserted on `banked`, the only observable that can tell the two orderings apart. Four reasons no offer is drawn, each a case: nothing installed (every target but the portal), adblocked, ONLINE (an ad freezes this client and lockstep cannot wait, `06`), or a run that carried nothing out (doubling zero is a button that lies). **The seam is the interesting half**: an offer is the one thing `PortalSession` cannot derive from the phase stream — everything else the portal wants is something it must be TOLD, an offer is something the player must be SHOWN — so the capability is declared game-side (`platform/rewardedAd.ts`, the `setUiAudio`/`setMusicAudio`/`setHostKind` pattern a fourth time), the portal supplies an adapter (`crazygames/portalRewardedAd.ts`), and the entry point installs it after `portal.start()` because that is where the probe runs. `src/game/` still imports nothing from `platform/crazygames/`. The adapter is a function and not an object literal in `main.crazygames.ts` for a reason worth generalising — an entry point runs `boot()` at import, so nothing written there is reachable by a test. **One rule the SDK's own cap does not cover**: a rewarded ad does not count against "one midgame every three minutes", so watching one and pressing CONFIRM handed the player a second ad seconds later — two legal calls, one outcome the requirements page rules out by name. `REWARDED_MIDGAME_COOLDOWN_MS` suppresses the automatic midgame for 60 s after a rewarded ad the player actually WATCHED, stamped from the outcome and not the request (an unfilled request cost no time and must not cost them the break ad). `Screens.ts` gains one optional `ResultOffer` with three promises — exists only when offered, one-shot, retired before the numbers change — and `claim()` resolves with lines in BOTH outcomes on purpose, because a player who pressed a button and saw nothing change cannot tell an unfilled ad from a broken button. The offer row is now the Screens variant `viewportFit` must clear, with the longest locale's label. **Verified live** on the portal dev build against the real SDK at `local`: a real tap, a real `requestAd`, the row reading `Materials banked: 8 (ad bonus x2)`, the bank `4 → 8`, the row closing again, `ticker.started` true afterwards (no leaked suspension), and a midgame immediately after coming back `{played:false, reason:'too-soon'}`. Client 5218 → **5264**, repo **8710** green, coverage 97.53%/92.57% over 243 files. `platform` `ui` `docs` `test`
- **09-07** [The report that closed by not happening](roadmap/42-2026-09-07-rewarded-ad.md#the-report-that-closed-by-not-happening-2026-09-07-docs-only-no-code-change) — *"拾取不到掉落物的报告 这个我这几天测试发现已经修复了"*. The unpickable-loot report, open since v47 and carried through four rounds of engine sweeps, is closed: **v51 was the report** (a locking door sealed the loot it closed over; re-clamped after `rebuildWalls`). **How it closed is the record worth keeping** — not by a measurement and not by the recorded run four rounds had been asking for, but by the ABSENCE of the symptom over several days of real play. Three stale scheduling sentences fell out, in the two places a resolved report leaves them: `design/18`'s two "Still open" notes and `current-state.md`'s "Whether v51 is *the* report is still unknown and still needs a recorded run". `ENGINE_VERSION_HISTORY.md`'s v51 entry needed nothing, which is the general point — a history entry that schedules nothing cannot go stale, and every correction here was to a sentence that promised future work. The sweeps were not wasted: four correct zeroes over 903 real drops are why v51's mechanism was THE remaining explanation rather than one guess among several. The replay ask stays right for the next report of this shape. `docs`
- **09-07** [The backup procedure stops being a sentence in a README](roadmap/42-2026-09-07-rewarded-ad.md#the-backup-procedure-stops-being-a-sentence-in-a-readme-2026-09-07-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"你自己决定，能做的就尽量做"*, and the highest-value thing an agent could do alone was the one item on the launch-readiness list that protects data rather than adding features. `accounts.db` (identity, ladder, meta) and `billing.db` (orders, entitlements, the outbox) hold the only two facts this project cannot regenerate, on one disk on a BORROWED box, and the backup procedure was two `scp` lines in a README — as reliable as somebody remembering it. **It is a fourth compose SERVICE, not a cron entry, and that decision carries the rest**: the CI deploy key runs one forced command installing `dist/` + `Dockerfile` + `docker-compose.yml`, so a service rides the deploy that exists while a host cron entry is a manual install nobody re-does — design/19 §9's own "a value written into an env file is not a value the process can see", one layer out. `server/src/backup/` is a fourth esbuild entrypoint (`backup.mjs`). Four properties, each with the test that fails when it is dropped: **`VACUUM INTO`, not `cp`** (a copy of a live database is a torn page set that opens fine and fails on the page that mattered — a row inserted between two cycles is proven to be in the second snapshot and not the first); **the worker cannot write to a live database** (probed, not assumed: `VACUUM INTO` works through a `readOnly: true` `node:sqlite` handle, so both data dirs are `:ro` mounts and the manifest test reads that `:ro` off the compose file); **a snapshot is verified before it is published** (`integrity_check` on the COPY, then gzip, then an atomic rename, so the directory never holds a file that merely looks like a backup); and **retention is per SOURCE and only advances on success** (a directory-wide count lets a busy database age out the other's history; pruning regardless of outcome turns a retention policy into a countdown to having nothing). `prunable` is pure with a case table because it is the one function here whose bug DESTROYS data — including that it ignores every file it did not write, since "delete the oldest files in the directory" deletes the hand-made copy somebody is examining. **The other half is being visible**: a portless worker is one nothing polls, so each cycle publishes `status.json` and the same bundle answers `node backup.mjs --health` as the container healthcheck — unhealthy when any source failed OR when the last cycle is too OLD, which is what stops a worker whose loop died after one good cycle from reporting green forever off a stale success. `ci-deploy.sh` now demands a healthy cycle after `docker compose up`, honouring its own comment that "a silently failed deploy is exactly as bad as a silently failed backup". It also REFUSES to start with no source (exit 2) rather than idling green, treating an empty env var as unset. **The manifest gate was generalised rather than widened**: three services were hardcoded in five places, and instead of deriving "HTTP service" from "has a PORT_VAR entry" it now carries explicit `HTTP_SERVICES` / `WORKER_SERVICES` lists, so a new service forces a decision about which kind it is — derive it and an HTTP service that forgot its port silently becomes a worker with no port assertions at all. `deploy.bundle.test.ts` runs the real bundle against a real seeded database, gunzips what it wrote and reads the row back. **One finding outside the task**: `ci-deploy.sh`'s header claimed `docker-compose.yml` is never touched and argued from it that the key cannot bind-mount the host's `/` into a container — the code copies it, so the one comment on that box's security posture was false and falsely reassuring. Corrected to describe the code, with the capability named and the exact change written down for whoever decides the borrowed box should not grant it; deliberately not "fixed" by dropping compose, which is the box owner's call and which this very pass depended on. Server 1031 → **1091**, repo **8770** green, coverage 98.90%/97.88% over 52 server files. **Still open and filed as such**: nothing is copied OFF the box — that needs a destination somebody owns. `platform` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-08 — the login the portal forbids, and the room it wants told about](roadmap/43-2026-09-08-portal-accounts.md)**

- **09-08** [The login page a portal forbids, and the onboarding that was never on the first-click path](roadmap/43-2026-09-08-portal-accounts.md#the-login-page-a-portal-forbids-and-the-onboarding-that-was-never-on-the-first-click-path-2026-09-08-client--server--docs-no-engine-change) — The premise the question came with (*"the server is deployed, so the normal game features need a login now"*) is not true of this code, and its being untrue is what makes the submission possible: `/find`, `/party/*` and `/rating/*` verify no token, and a guest plays online exactly as before. What IS true is that `docs.crazygames.com/requirements/account-integration` forbids the login screen we ship — external login options (it names email), a logout that leads back to one, a login button as a primary CTA — and REQUIRES that a logged-in portal user be registered and signed in automatically. So `MainMenu.setAccountEntry(false)` + an unwired `onAccount` make the screen unreachable on that host (`storePlatform.ts`'s precedent: no entry rendered, never one drawn and refusing), and `platform/crazygames/portalAuth.ts` signs the player in from the entry point instead — availability first, a guest is a normal outcome, `showAuthPrompt` declared and deliberately never called. `POST /auth/portal` is the trust boundary: `portalToken.ts` verifies RS256 with `node:crypto` (no JWT dependency, `ticket.ts`'s precedent) and checks `alg` as an EQUALITY before touching the key, with `alg: none` and `HS256`-signed-with-the-public-key as their own cases. The owner's decision that one human is two accounts is what the schema encodes: handle `{provider}:{providerId}` (unreachable by `validateUsername`, so `alice` local and `alice` portal cannot collide), a new `display_name` column because a provider's name cannot be forced through our charset/blacklist without creating players who can never log in, and a `password_hash` sentinel checked EXPLICITLY plus a `provider !== 'local'` refusal in `login` — two independent guards, the second proven by planting a working hash. First real migration (`PRAGMA table_info` guard; tested against the old DDL on a real temp file, since `:memory:` always takes the fresh-schema path). Onboarding: volume 41's one-click PLAY had also made the first-click path the one with no control instructions on it, so `beginRun` now arms the teaching beats for a player who has never been taught — with a lesson this loadout cannot teach SKIPPED rather than waited on, and per-device wording that finally names the real keys instead of an aim stick design/10 v33 removed. `platform` `net` `ui` `test` `docs`
- **09-08** [The room the portal wants told about, and the name every other client draws](roadmap/43-2026-09-08-portal-accounts.md#the-room-the-portal-wants-told-about-and-the-name-every-other-client-draws-2026-09-08-client--server--engine-protocol--docs-no-engine-bump) — The other decision (multiplayer stays in the first submission) makes `requirements/multiplayer` apply, and nothing had touched it. Our joinable unit is a PARTY, never a live match room whose lockstep seats cannot absorb a joiner — so `platform/partyPresence.ts` carries `{partyId, code, joinable}` out of `PartyScreen` and `PortalRooms.ts` turns it into `updateRoom`/`leftRoom` plus the portal's own invite button. This is the one thing `PortalSession` cannot derive from the phase stream, and why is worth stating: a phase says which SCREEN is open, and the interesting transitions (a fourth member arriving, the leader pressing START) change no phase at all. `joinable` is reported honestly against the same cap the server refuses a join by, because the platform draws an affordance off that answer. `platform/onlineEntry.ts` is the capability an accepted invite and `isInstantMultiplayer` are acted on through — neither could be a query param, since every other boot-time entry is read inside `Game`'s constructor and the SDK answers after it — and an invite BEATS instant multiplayer, because a queue for strangers would discard the only information the link carried. The names half needed a channel this game never had: no player name existed anywhere in the netcode. It rides design/16's `accountId` path one field over (`TicketPayload.name` → `Matchmaker` → `Seat` → `MatchStart.names`), and `/find` now takes it from the bearer session it verifies — the body's `accountId` is IGNORED when a session is present, because a name is the one field other players see and a self-declared one is an impersonation primitive. A room with no logged-in players puts nothing new on the wire (`undefined`, not an array of nulls); a mixed room nulls seats at their own index rather than compacting, since position IS the seat index; `conn_resync` carries it too, because a reconnecting client never sees `match_start` again. Displayed as `ui/SeatRoster.ts` — one line, not nameplates (four labels over four 20-pixel sprites, fighting the occlusion x-ray) and not the ally row, which an online co-op match never draws. `platform` `net` `ui` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-08 — going live: the backup that never ran, and the guest that was a broken SDK](roadmap/44-2026-09-08-go-live.md)**

- **09-08** [The backup that never ran, and the check that existed only in the repo](roadmap/44-2026-09-08-go-live.md#the-backup-that-never-ran-and-the-check-that-existed-only-in-the-repo-2026-09-08-server--deploy--ci--docs-no-engine-change) — Two things that went wrong were both things a green pipeline was already reporting as fine. The instruction to snapshot before the `display_name` migration was right for the wrong reason: both databases held **0 rows in every table**, so the risk volume 43 named did not exist yet — a green migration over an empty table is not evidence about a full one, so count rows before calling one production-proven. What the snapshot did find is that the daily backup worker from volume 42 had **never produced a snapshot in 18 hours**, from two faults that each would have caught the other. A bind mount HIDES the image's `chown`: Docker creates a missing mount source as `root:root` and the mount then replaces the image's directory, ownership included, so the container user (uid 1000) died `EACCES` on every write and the restart loop was `--health`'s staleness arm working as designed with nobody watching. And the `ci-deploy.sh` backup poll that would have failed that deploy existed only in the REPO — the live copy is hand-installed on purpose (the CI key must not rewrite its own forced command) and never was, so the deploy ran the old script and went green in 46s under a comment reading *"a silently failed deploy is exactly as bad as a silently failed backup"*. The transferable half: **for any file whose live copy is deliberately not deployable, CI going green is not evidence the new check ran** — the answer is a `diff`, now in the README. Fixed by construction on the first fault (idempotent ownership normalisation before `compose up`, with `deploy.manifests.test.ts` parsing compose's `volumes:` and pinning the chown list to the real mounts plus the uid to the Dockerfile's `USER node`); the second got the recipe, the incident written down, and the script installed. Go-live checks then passed with their CONTROLS in frame: `/find` + Bearer carries `"name"`, without the header the ticket has no `name` key at all, and a body `accountId` is discarded when a session is present.
- **09-08** [A hosted policy, and the guest that was really a broken SDK](roadmap/44-2026-09-08-go-live.md#a-hosted-policy-and-the-guest-that-was-really-a-broken-sdk-2026-09-08-client--docs-no-engine-change) — Two of design/20's named gaps. The hosted privacy policy and terms are live at `b.gamestao.com/privacy` and `/terms`, with the entity facts taken from the sibling `funny` project but the CONTENT rewritten rather than adapted, because a copied policy would overstate collection: no email is collected anywhere, no analytics exists in the tree (the grep for it matched only the audio term *amplitude*), and no payment processor is connected — verified against the live deployment, where `/webhook/dev` is 404 and `/store/skus` is 401, so the dev stub cannot mint a free entitlement from outside. The rule that outlived the gap moved from prose into a type: `policyUrl` returns `string | null`. Its first version was decoration — the null arm was unreachable, which is a coverage hole and a design smell arriving together — so `isUsablePolicyUrl` now refuses anything not absolute `http(s)`, which is this target's specific trap: a relative `/privacy` resolves against the PORTAL's host inside its frame, 404ing for exactly the players the link is required for while passing every check run on our own domain. The bigger finding is that **`__portal.diagnostics()` was blind precisely where the danger is**: step 5 said to watch for `NOT signed in (<reason>)`, and that arm cannot fire for a `getUser` that fails, because `readUser` goes through `settle()` whose whole contract is that a failure is indistinguishable from "returned nothing" — so a thrown `getUser` printed `guest` with a null reason. `getUser` is in BETA on the platform's side, making that the likeliest failure of the entire silent-login path, and it would have looked like a quiet day. Fixed without relaxing that invariant: `settleReporting` keeps the reason, `readUserReporting` also counts an un-narrowable answer as failed (the renamed-field case, already live once), and a fifth state `getUser BROKEN (<reason>)` is checked BEFORE the guest arm because the two share a null user and a guard after it would be unreachable. Nothing branches on it — every path still ends at a playable guest. Also: `isUserAccountAvailable` is already true on the unregistered `local` domain, so checking it on the real one proves nothing, and the SUB-PATH serve `launch.json` had described as necessary for a year is finally runnable — 82 same-origin requests, 0 failures, nothing outside the sub-path.

**[2026-09-08 — the codename's last surface](roadmap/45-2026-09-08-env-prefix.md)**

- **09-08** [The codename's last surface](roadmap/45-2026-09-08-env-prefix.md#the-codenames-last-surface-2026-09-08-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"现在游戏有自己的名字了，改为BB开头的吧"*: the `DDU_*` environment prefix is the last family of identifiers still carrying the codename, and volume 39's rename rule is why it was left — machine-matched names do not move. It is also the one machine-matched name in the tree whose match is **re-bindable at zero cost**, which is a third category the 2026-09-06 list did not have: the other side of the match is one file an operator owns, so rebinding it loses no save, rejects no replay and orphans no custom domain. 22 names across 66 files, 314 lines, line for line — no behaviour, no test added or removed, 9066 green. Two things are worth more than the rename. **The gate that made it a `sed` rather than an audit hardcodes the string being renamed**: `deploy.manifests.test.ts` requires every compose env name to be read somewhere in `src/`, and one of its three scan patterns is the prefix literal `/'(BB_[A-Z0-9_]+)'/` (there because `startupGuard`'s `DEV_ONLY_FLAGS` reaches its names through `env[name]`) — a checker whose implementation contains the string under rename is the shape that goes quietly blind, and this one only happens to fail safe. And **the whole risk is two lines in the one file CI is forbidden to ship**: `.env` holds the ticket secret and the internal key, and their failures are asymmetric in the wrong direction — an unset `BB_INTERNAL_KEY` fails closed and loudly, while an unset `BB_TICKET_SECRET` falls back to the *published dev secret* with a warning and keeps working, a live-server downgrade whose only symptom is looking healthy. Since `env_file` injects names and not a schema, a `.env` carrying both prefixes is read correctly by both codes, so the migration is additive-then-subtractive with no window — both commands in `server/deploy/README.md`. Not moved, and now annotated so the next sweep does not finish the job: the map editor's three `ddu-mapeditor:*` autosave keys, matched against a key already in a designer's browser. `platform` `docs`

**[2026-09-08 — the frame nobody sees: a power budget, a middle rung, and a frame rate](roadmap/46-2026-09-08-power-budget.md)**

- **09-08** [The frame nobody sees, and the 120 Hz nobody asked for](roadmap/46-2026-09-08-power-budget.md#the-frame-nobody-sees-and-the-120-hz-nobody-asked-for-2026-09-08-client-only-no-engine-change) — *"游戏现在运行在手机和ipad上时耗电量非常高"*, and what it named was not a slow frame: **every instrument here measures framerate, and nothing measured waste.** Two of them. Outside `'playing'` **nothing in `layers.world` is visible and all of it was still being drawn** — every menu, the pause menu and both result screens are backed by the same opaque full-viewport panel art, and nothing unmounts the room when a run ENDS (`resetRenderState` runs at the start of the next one), so a player sitting in the forge paid a complete in-run frame (29 ground pieces, 64 entity views, four filter passes) at the display's refresh rate for as long as they sat there: 42 draws/18 programs, and hiding `layers.world` moved **0 of 329,160 pixels**. And **`app.ticker.maxFPS` was 0**, so a 120 Hz ProMotion iPad drew FOUR frames per 30 Hz sim tick — `grep maxFPS` over the client matched nothing outside `src/perf/`. `game/powerBudget.ts` fixes both as one function of `phase`: menu 11 draws → **3**, pause 40 → **4**, forge-after-a-run 42 → **7**, 60 fps in a run and 30 elsewhere. The finding about the INSTRUMENT outlives the numbers: `frameProbe`'s default liveness control blanks the whole stage, so it fires whenever anything is drawn — including a frame that is entirely opaque menu — and every "0 pixels changed" reading came back `trustworthy: true` meaning nothing; hiding `layers.ui` instead moved 99.1% and is what separates *drawn and covered* from *not drawn*. Two `pixi.js` `Ticker` facts needed tests of their own (the cap gate truncates elapsed time to an integer, so 60 on a 60 Hz panel has an obvious way to halve everyone's frame rate; and `set maxFPS` silently lowers `minFPS`, which is the post-stall `deltaMS` clamp). The CPU half stayed, measured and deliberately: `update 0.4 ms` against `render 1.2 ms`. `perf` `render`
- **09-08** [A middle rung on the ladder, and a frame rate the player picks](roadmap/46-2026-09-08-power-budget.md#a-middle-rung-on-the-ladder-and-a-frame-rate-the-player-picks-2026-09-08-client--i18n-no-engine-change) — The owner picked both remaining levers. **`medium` is a rung, not "low with lighting"**: it keeps the one `sceneLight` pass and drops the three stacked above it, so a frame goes from four full-viewport render-target passes to one — draws 41/34/32 and **framebuffer binds 11/3/1** across high/medium/low, and the bind line is the one a mobile tiler charges for. Its `resolutionCap` stays at high's 2 for a reason worth keeping: Pixi's `Filter.resolution` defaults to 1 and does not follow the renderer's, so the world is ALREADY rasterized at 1x everywhere and lowering the renderer resolution buys only the unfiltered layers — the HUD and the menu text. `resolveTier` is a ladder now and `QualityWatchdog` steps instead of latching (`downgrades: number`, streak restarting per rung, bounded by `maxSteps`): "high does not fit" is no evidence about medium, so dropping straight to the cheapest tier would cost every mid-range phone the lighting it could afford — reaching `low` off the real sampler takes ~12 s of slow windows rather than 6. Plus a **FRAME RATE setting (60/30)**, 30 being one render frame per sim tick, on a module mirror because the reader is the main loop, landing in `SettingsBinding.applyAll` so it applies at boot AND on change AND on the ad-mute re-apply. Two cross-constraints became assertions: the idle cap may not fall below the watchdog's 25 fps floor (30 never trips it, 20 does — measured against the real sampler), and an idle screen may never cost more frames than the run. Three i18n keys × eight locales, and three source comments that COUNTED tiers had to be rephrased to name the property instead. `render` `ui` `i18n`
- **09-08** [The premises a perf fix rests on, and the test that was vacuous](roadmap/46-2026-09-08-power-budget.md#the-premises-a-perf-fix-rests-on-and-the-test-that-was-vacuous-2026-09-08-client-tests-only-no-code-change) — An 18-mutant battery, 18 killed and both controls surviving — after two rounds, and the two things the first round found are worth more than the score. **One survivor was a genuinely vacuous test of mine**: `maxFpsForPhase` returns `min(IDLE_MAX_FPS, playCap)`, `IDLE_MAX_FPS` is 30 and the lowest offered rate is 30, so every assertion written from a legal value passes whether the `min` exists or not — deleting it survived 5,500 tests, the dominant survivor cause this log already records (the fixture made two different things equal), fixed by driving the mirror past its own type. The other was a **no-op mutant** whose SURVIVED line said nothing; re-run as real edits it died instantly. Both premises of the fix are now tests rather than one browser reading: `menuCoversWorld.test.ts` (every menu-shaped screen mounts a full-bleed opaque sprite over the whole viewport — mocking `getUiTexture` is what makes the shipped branch reachable, since a `Panel` with no art is a translucent scrim) and a `layers.test.ts` parent-chain case (every layer a run draws into is under `world`). Adding the only two screens `viewportFit.test.ts` had ever been missing — `Matchmaking` and `StoreScreen`, missing because both do async work in `show()`, which left the queue and the store unchecked against a 390 px phone — exposed that **a fits-the-viewport sweep is blind to the bug it was written for**: a bottom-anchored control always "fits" (the store's BACK sits at `h - 56`, so its content ends 24 px above the bottom at every height), an overlap is not an overflow, and **±Infinity passes both bounds assertions** so the suite passed hardest on the emptiest screen. The guard that works is the BUILDER asserting its own precondition — deleting the settle point then failed 18 cases instead of 1. `test` `ui`

**[2026-09-09 — one store for both halves: the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md)**

- **09-09** [One store for both halves — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"在后端的container里，集成grafana...并用于收集和展示前端的日志"*. design/19 §8 said not to do this, and the row was right when written — what changed is the premise, not the argument: there is a live backend and a public client on three targets now, so there is a sink worth having. **The finding that reframed it came before a line was written**: the borrowed box already runs its owner's full Grafana/Loki/Promtail/Prometheus stack, their promtail scrapes the Docker socket UNFILTERED, so every line our containers ever wrote was already in their store — and it still holds `blightbloom-*` streams from a short-lived container naming on 09-07, i.e. the game's name reached that shared box despite the naming policy, and log CONTENT carries it regardless of the container name. That set the constraint that mattered most: **every service name here is `obs-`prefixed**, because this project joins the host's shared `docker_default` and compose publishes each service NAME as a network alias on it — a service called `loki` would put two containers behind one DNS name, their collector's pushes landing in our store or ours in theirs, intermittently, with nothing failing anywhere. Shipped: four off-the-shelf containers (Loki 14-day, Alloy socket-read-only and filtered to `wnet-test-*`, Prometheus, Grafana at `/grafana/` behind its own login), `src/log.ts` (`HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL [tag] msg k=v` — human-readable because `docker compose logs` is still what anybody reaches for first, and a formatter that guarantees ONE line and ONE logfmt token per value so call sites cannot break the collector's parse), a `/metrics` route per service, and `POST /client/log`. Five decisions each with a way they would have gone wrong: labels are a fixed tiny set with everything identifying in the LINE (a label per session id is one stream per player per visit); telemetry may never affect a player (every outcome is `200 {ok, accepted}` — a 4xx teaches a client to retry forever — and the push is `void`-ed, tested against a deliberately slow stub); **a device clock is not a clock**, so a client timestamp becomes an AGE bounded against the SERVER's clock (a wrong clock otherwise drops the batch AT the store with a 400 nobody here is watching for), in `BigInt` nanoseconds because `ms * 1e6` goes exponential past 1e21 and Loki rejects it; and the account id is resolved from the bearer token server-side, never read from the body. From funny, verbatim and highest-value: `fetch(keepalive, credentials: 'omit')` and **never** `sendBeacon`, which always sends credentialed and so cannot be combined with our `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — get it wrong and the exit flush, the only chance to hear about a crash, silently never lands. The client's ~100 existing `console.error`/`warn` sites are captured by WRAPPING the two methods rather than rewriting them: a missed call site is a class of error that never reports and nothing goes red. A 5-minute `info` heartbeat, because an idle log store and a broken one draw the same empty dashboard. Two deploy-shaped catches: matchsvc's `/metrics` would have been PUBLIC (it is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — refused now by the presence of `x-forwarded-for`, with a 404 rather than a 403), and a missing `BB_GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` stops the whole deploy on purpose (no fallback is available when the image default is admin/admin on a public login page). New gate `deploy.observability.test.ts` runs **Alloy's regex against a line the real logger produces** — six config files referencing each other through text no compiler compares — which is also how the dashboards' "All" level turned out to need `.*` not `.+` (an unparsed line has no `level` label, and an absent label matches only the empty string). `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-09 — retention, and the sentence a policy stopped being able to say](roadmap/48-2026-09-09-analytics.md)**

- **09-09** [Retention, and the sentence a policy stopped being able to say](roadmap/48-2026-09-09-analytics.md#retention-and-the-sentence-a-policy-stopped-being-able-to-say-2026-09-09-client--server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"funny的后台管理系统，我们这边应该也需要，只是不需要那么多功能。主要的用户管理和留存打点肯定是要的"*, then *"隐私政策改说明。暴露到公网。先文档再按顺序实现"*. The ask was `funny`'s ops backend (5,285 + 6,756 lines); almost none of it is here, and **why not is worth more than what is**. Two things already in place shrank it to a third: volume 47 landed the log store the same morning, so the monitoring half was answered — and **Grafana IS an ops frontend**, already deployed behind its own login, which is why `funny` hand-wrote 6,756 lines of one and this does not. That fixed the scoping rule for [design/21-ops-analytics.md](21-ops-analytics.md): anything expressible as a time series goes to Prometheus and is drawn by Grafana; a bespoke page is justified only for what a time series cannot express, and **exactly one thing qualifies — a row per player**. The owner asked for the console on the public internet, which raises the bar rather than lowering it, and the answer is the decision the rest hangs off: **it is read-only over player data, and every player-data mutation stays a CLI script on the box**, where SSH access is already the second factor. That single choice deletes RBAC, the four-role matrix, the compensation approval workflow and the audit-visibility split — the largest block of `funny`'s admin — because there are no writes to gate; and it supersedes design/19 §7's *"No admin service… revisit when the first refund arrives"* by a different trigger than the one it named. **Phase A shipped**: one shared vocabulary imported by both sides through `@dd/net/*` (two copies that drift produce no type error and no red test, only an event sent forever and dropped forever), `POST /client/events` as a sibling of `/client/log` sharing its limiter and its always-200 contract, a third SQLite database, an hourly rollup whose gauges land on the existing `/metrics`, a Grafana dashboard, and the client SDK. Four load-bearing decisions: `daily_active(day, install)` is its own table so retention does not DEPEND on the prune (otherwise the day the events window drops a week, D7 for that week silently becomes `0` rather than unknown) and so DAU-by-host is a PARTITION that sums to DAU; the rollup **writes before it exposes**, because Prometheus keeps 15 days and a gauge cannot be backfilled; **an unaged cohort is ABSENT, never zero** — a gauge has no "unknown", and in a launch week every offset is unaged, where a 0 would read as *nobody ever came back*; and collection is opt-in by the same env var the backup worker discovers the file by, so "collected" and "backed up" cannot disagree. **The install id turned out to already exist** — `daydayup.playerId.v1`, stored since the PvP squad work — so analytics adds no new stored identifier and retention is continuous from day one; what it could not reuse is `getPlayerId()`, which prefers the account id and would change a player's identity mid-cohort when they log in. Three events are **derived from the frame** rather than announced from the fifteen places that write `RunState.phase`, which also makes **abandonment detected rather than reported** — so a quit route nobody thought of is still counted. Two vocabulary entries came back OUT for the same reason (a declared field nothing can populate is an always-empty panel): `run_start.weapon`, because no authored weapon id survives into the sim state, and `store_open`, because opening the store IS a `screen_view`. **The privacy policy could no longer say what it said** — and the bullet was not the problem, the callout was: *"Playing without an account collects nothing on our server"* is exactly false for analytics, since a guest is who retention is about. Found while rewriting it: **the localStorage list was already wrong**, naming three items while the shipped game has written a fourth all along. Every number in it is now a number the code deletes on (90 days / 180 / 14, the last checked against the log store's own config rather than asserted), and the cohort window came down from 400 days to 180 because a policy has to state the number and the number has to be defensible. **One host is deliberately excluded and that is the interesting part**: WeChat has no `localStorage`, so the install id would be minted per visit — retention would read 0% instead of absent, but **DAU would report VISITS while labelled distinct installs**, which is worse, because it is a plausible number nobody would question. No data beats wrong data. **Running it found two defects neither the tests nor the review did.** Every abandoned run reported no floor and no duration — quitting tears the engine down, so the props were read from a state already `null`, and the one number the event exists for was missing from every row; no test could have caught it, because at that seam the state is an argument. And **every flush paid its own CORS preflight, including the exit one** (no `access-control-max-age` on the shared block) — the one request that cannot be retried, since `keepalive` protects a request already STARTED and an uncached preflight makes the `pagehide` flush two round trips behind an unloading document, i.e. exactly the `session_end` funny lost outright. Both fixed and verified in live traffic. New gate `deploy.dashboardMetrics.test.ts`: every metric a panel queries must be one the server emits, because a dashboard is the one artefact whose broken state looks exactly like its working state — proven the same day by volume 47's own fully-populated, meaningless panel. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-09 — a console that cannot write, and a flag that does nothing](roadmap/49-2026-09-09-ops-console.md)**

- **09-09** [A console that cannot write, and a flag that does nothing](roadmap/49-2026-09-09-ops-console.md#a-console-that-cannot-write-and-a-flag-that-does-nothing-2026-09-09-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"继续"*, against design/21 §7's own order. Phase B is a FIFTH process (`server/src/adminsvc/`, 8790) rather than routes on matchsvc, because matchsvc is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — a route added there is public the moment it exists, which is why `/metrics` had to explicitly refuse proxied requests, and a panel built there would make every future admin route inherit *public unless it remembers not to be*. The property that makes a publicly-exposed console proportionate is the FORM of decision B1: **it is not that the console does not write player data, it is that it cannot** — `readOnly: true` handles on all three databases, plus `:ro` bind mounts in compose, so it is refused once by SQLite and once by the kernel. Asserted the only way that claim can be: a test ATTEMPTS an INSERT, an UPDATE, a DELETE and a DROP through each handle and requires each to throw, with a `SELECT` beside them as the control (checking the option was passed would pass against a handle that ignored it). **Every handle is nullable and none of the nulls is defensive**: `readOnly` does not create a missing file, and all three belong to other processes — so the console comes up with per-section "unavailable" cards carrying the reason, proven by booting the real bundle with none of the three present, because a process that refused to start could not be used to find out why the file is not there. **The design named the wrong table for one column**: §3.2 said last-active comes from `daily_active`, which holds no account id at all — by design, since a cohort is a question about a browser and joining it to an account would assemble exactly the profile §2.1 promises not to. It comes from `events.account_id`, which is pruned at 90 days, so a blank cell means *no event in the window* and never *never played* — and the page says so, because a column that conflated those would be read as the second one. The retention grid carries volume 48's rule one layer out: an unaged cohort arrives as a MISSING ROW, so `CohortCell` is `{rate,size} | null` and the fixture is built by running the shipped `persistRollup` once per simulated day, giving a measured 50%, a measured 0% and an unknown side by side; the page prints the rollup row count too, since an empty grid over zero rows is an empty database and over three hundred is a bug in the reader. **The XSS this console is really exposed to is `webhook_events.raw`** — bytes an outsider POSTed to the billing plane, rendered in the browser of the one account that can read every player's row — so the view modules deliberately do NOT pre-escape (no "already safe" category to reason about), `esc` is the single gate, and the test drives the whole chain from the real billing schema through the real read-only handle to the rendered page. A test also caught a live bug: the cohort tooltip read "4 of 4 installs returned" on a 50% cell, because `daily_rollup` stores the rate and the size and **not** the return count — a tooltip that contradicts its own percentage is the kind of instrument that gets believed. **Phase C's finding is a switch that does nothing.** Two of the four flags are about the CLIENT, and §4's delivery mechanism is an `x-internal-key` endpoint a browser cannot call and must never be able to — so they have a row, a control, and nothing on the other end. This project has paid for that shape once (volume 47's fully-populated, meaningless panel), so the state is in the TYPE, rendered as a `not delivered` badge, and pinned by a test so it cannot be "fixed" by flipping the boolean instead of building the path. **A flag captured at construction is not a flag**: `Matchmaker` held its two timings as numbers, so a console change would only land on the next restart; they are suppliers now, read per decision. C1 is enforced on BOTH sides of the table (`setFlag` will not write an unknown name, `readOverrides` will not return one — the two are edited by different people at different times), the poll parse is all-or-nothing (a partial merge lets a garbled response revert a deliberate override because a *different* flag was corrupt), and the allowlist test pins the exact set of names on purpose: it is the mechanism, not a check. Every new module is at **100% lines and branches** except the entry point's `require.main` guard, and getting there DELETED five branches rather than testing them — a branch no input can reach is one a coverage gate cannot tell from an untested one, which is the shape that gets removed in a refactor with nothing going red. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-09 — the switch that did nothing, wired to something](roadmap/50-2026-09-09-client-flag-delivery.md)**

- **09-09** [The switch that did nothing, wired to something](roadmap/50-2026-09-09-client-flag-delivery.md#the-switch-that-did-nothing-wired-to-something-2026-09-09-client--server--docs-no-engine-change) — *"继续"*, closing the one thing [design/21-ops-analytics.md](21-ops-analytics.md) §9 filed as missing plus three of its four open questions. **§9 proposed a shape that does not exist.** The plan was a public field on "a response the client already fetches", chosen to avoid adding a public surface — and a browser makes exactly two unauthenticated calls to matchsvc, both 30-second batched, fire-and-forget, absent on the WeChat shell, and one of them behind an opt-out §9's own consent question would switch off. That is fine for the ad switch (read when a run ends) and no use at all for a notice telling a player the servers are going down, and it would couple an operational switch to the analytics opt-in. So `GET /client/flags` on matchsvc: unauthenticated, `no-store`, answering from the values that process already polls, reading NO database, not rate-limited (`/health` beside it is the precedent — no work, no state, and a per-IP limit meets a school NAT long before an attacker), and answering proxied requests happily, which is the exact OPPOSITE of `/metrics` in the same dispatch chain — asserted with `/metrics` as its control, because a copy-paste of that guard produces a route that works from a test and 404s for every real player. **The contract lives in the CLIENT tree** (`client/src/net/publicFlags.ts`, imported by the server through `@dd/net/*` as `analyticsEvents.ts` already is), because the client is the half that cannot be redeployed in lockstep — so a value it may receive must have a compiled-in meaning on the day it arrives. Each default, the banner cap and the forbidden character class are therefore ONE literal, and the contract test drives BOTH validators over the same strings: a server accepting a banner the client refuses is a notice set in the console and invisible in the game with nothing anywhere saying why. **`FlagDef.public` is only half a marker** — a flag reaches a browser only if it is `public: true` on the server AND in the client's contract, two edits in two workspaces, absent meaning private, so publishing cannot happen as a side effect of adding. And the test of whether a flag MAY be public is not "is it harmless" but **"is its value already visible to the player it is delivered to"**: the banner IS its own disclosure, the ad offer is a button on their screen, and `match.pvpBotBackfillDelayMs` — no secret, and still not ours to hand out — would tell a player which opponent was not a person. `publicFlagValues` is an explicit literal rather than a filter-and-cast so a forgotten name is a COMPILE error; an `Object.fromEntries(...) as PublicFlags` would type-check whatever it produced and ship a name missing, which the all-or-nothing parse then refuses wholesale, leaving every browser on defaults with nothing red. **All-or-nothing gives the path a deploy ORDERING** (a client knowing a name the server does not yet send falls back for ALL of them, so a third public flag ships SERVER-first, or one window runs with every override off including a banner just put up), and **a stale response must not win**: a monotonic generation counter drops any answer a newer fetch superseded, checked BEFORE the health update — dropping only a stale SUCCESS would still let a stalled poll's failure mark the client unhealthy and warn after a newer poll had succeeded, a log line contradicting the values in use. Five minutes, not the services' sixty seconds, because a client poll's cost scales with PLAYERS; the warning is a transition and is silent before the first success, so a dev client with no server and every offline player write nothing. Two consumers: a FIFTH refusal in `RunOutcome.doubleOffer`, read per offer rather than at install (an install-time check is a switch needing a reload — `Matchmaker`'s captured timings again), pinned by a test using ONE `RunOutcome` across a flip because a fresh one cannot tell the implementations apart; and a `MainMenu` banner that does not affect the layout (fixed offset above the title, bottom-anchored so wrapping grows upward, positioned even while hidden), is NOT localised and cannot be (there is no key for a line an operator typed), and is stroked rather than carded because a backing Panel would have to read `Text.height` and force a canvas measurement. `gameWiring.ts` subscribes the menu to the store — ONE listener slot, notified only on an actual CHANGE — so a notice set while a player is already in the menu appears without them navigating away. **The SWEEP found the bug nothing else could**: adding a `MainMenu (maintenance banner)` entry to `viewportFit.test.ts` failed at five of seven viewports at once, because `wordWrap` alone breaks at SPACES and a 140-character banner with none cannot wrap at all — a perfectly legal value, since the contract refuses markup and control characters, not long words. `breakWords` is the fix, and the general shape is that the sweep was structurally BLIND to the state until the state was put in front of it: built by `new MainMenu(); show()`, it read an empty flag store and never drew a banner. **Driven live, and the first check lied**: the whole loop runs with nothing hand-assembled — a value typed into the console's form reached `ops.db`, matchsvc's 60s poll logged it, the public route served it carrying ONLY the two public names, and the real menu drew it above the title, then a change made while the menu was on screen arrived through the client's own 5-minute poll with no reload. A non-ASCII banner round-trips byte-identically, but the first attempt reported a `U+FFFD` — the harness, not the code: this shell sends an em dash as cp1252, so the server correctly decoded invalid UTF-8. The console now also says the thing an operator must know before typing: a `public` flag's value is PUBLISHED and not merely applied, and browsers poll on a different cadence from the services — without which "I set the banner and it is not showing" is a real report with no bug behind it. **Three of §9's four questions closed by decision**: no EU consent banner (legitimate interest stands; the gate stays a call site because `setAnalytics(null)` is already the default, and the flag path deliberately does not ride on analytics), Prometheus retention ACCEPTED at 15 days (Prometheus owns infrastructure time series, `daily_rollup` owns retention history, because a number answerable from two stores with two windows is a number two people can disagree about), and the delivery path itself. `net` `ui` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-09 — the two globals a mini-game does not have](roadmap/51-2026-09-09-wechat-network-adapter.md)**

- **09-09** [The two globals a mini-game does not have, and the four features waiting on them](roadmap/51-2026-09-09-wechat-network-adapter.md#the-two-globals-a-mini-game-does-not-have-and-the-four-features-waiting-on-them-2026-09-09-client--docs-no-engine-change) — *"implement the WeChat IdentityStore over wx.getStorageSync/setStorageSync ... plus a wx.request shim for fetch ... then install analytics and confirm public flags stop being inert there"*. Volume 48 left this host out of analytics and volume 50 left its flag poll inert; both filed the same fix, and the two absences behind it turn out to be different KINDS of gap. No `fetch` is an **absence** — no banner, the ad switch at its compiled default, indistinguishable from an unreachable server, which is why installing the poll early was right and delivery arrived as one argument on a call already in the right place. No `localStorage` was a **wrong number waiting to happen**: every analytics row is keyed by the install id, whose store reads a global this shell does not have, so a FRESH id was minted per visit — retention would read 0% instead of absent (bad) and **DAU would report VISITS while labelled distinct installs** (worse, because nobody questions a plausible number). That decided the order of the work: the storage half is the point, the `fetch` shim is what makes it worth anything. `IdentityStore` was already the seam and had no way to be REACHED — the two production readers take no arguments — so `setIdentityStore` joins `setAssetHost`/`setHostKind`/`setUiAudio` as a module sink, dropping both id caches on the way in (an id already handed out came from the store installed then, and answering with it makes the swap a lie), and the entry point calls it BEFORE `installAnalytics`, because the install id is read once DURING the install and a store swapped in afterwards is indistinguishable in the data from the bug. The shim's edges are where the work was, each failing silently toward a plausible answer: **`wx.request` defaults to `dataType: 'json'`**, which pre-parses the body and hands back `undefined` for anything that is not JSON — the flag module rests on an HTML error page being a FAILED parse, so the raw string is requested and the test asserts the default was overridden; a **404 must RESOLVE** with `ok: false` the way `fetch` does, since that is the case separated from a transport failure; a `fail` callback AND a synchronous throw must both reject, or a caller's `await` hangs forever; and headers must survive, because the one that matters is `authorization` (funny shipped 2,848 unattributable `session_end` rows for a related reason). `credentials: 'omit'`/`keepalive` are accepted and IGNORED — a wildcard CORS origin and an unloading page are both load-bearing on the web and meaningless where there is no origin and no page. **`session_end` stays absent here on purpose**: this runtime never fires `pagehide`, and `wx.onHide` is not a substitute — it fires on every backgrounding and is followed by `onShow`, so routing it in would multiply the row the churn funnel counts and understate every duration; it drives the FLUSH alone, which is the honest half, and matters because a backgrounded mini-game can be killed with no further notice. Every test carries its control, and the controls are the old bugs: the flag case is paired with the same shell and no shim (values as compiled in, nothing sent), and the two-visit install id is paired with the default web store in a runner that has no `localStorage`, which still produces two distinct ids — the number that would have been DAU. What no test can reach is the pair of OPERATIONAL gates: `wx.request` refuses plain http and a plain `npm run build:wechat` bakes in `localhost:8788` (no CI build for this target), and the host must be in the account's 服务器域名 whitelist, which fails on a handset while DevTools with 不校验域名 ticked succeeds. Both are silent and fail-safe, so **zero `wechat` rows in `daily_active` is the symptom of both of them and of a build nobody played, and the events store cannot tell them apart** — filed as design/04 checklist item 18 as a POSITIVE check. **Wiring it up exposed a label that had been wrong for as long as the entry existed**: `hostKind` defaults to `web`, only the portal entry ever called `setHostKind`, and this entry carried a comment asserting it did not need to. Invisible while `isPortalHost()` was the only reader (false either way) — and `clientLog` is a reader of a different KIND, one that does not switch on the value but LABELS every batch with it, which the server allowlists into a Loki stream label. So a missing declaration is not a no-op falling back to a safe default: it is every WeChat failure filed under `web` with `host="wechat"` matching nothing, forever. `main.ts` now declares `web` too (a default an entry silently relies on cannot be told from one a new entry forgot) and `hostKind.test.ts` sweeps all three, because a single-entry check only exists where somebody thought of it — which is exactly how this was missed. `meta/store.ts` and `settings/store.ts` stay on the in-memory store: the primitive is exported for them, what is left is each store's own migration question. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-09 → 09-10 — the deploy that reported success three times, and `main` stops being committable](roadmap/52-2026-09-09-backend-deploy.md)**

- **09-09** [The deploy that reported success three times while nothing changed](roadmap/52-2026-09-09-backend-deploy.md#the-deploy-that-reported-success-three-times-while-nothing-changed-2026-09-09-deploy--server--docs-no-engine-change) — Volumes 47–50 built the observability stack, analytics, the ops console and client flag delivery and left all of it unpushed; this is the push. Nine containers now run on the borrowed VPS and every row of `server/deploy/README.md` §4 has been walked against the live host — but what the volume records is that **three separate things reported success having done nothing**, each by a different mechanism, and that the runbook predicted one of them with the right symptom and the wrong cause. `platform` `docs`
- **09-10** [`main` stops being a place you can commit to](roadmap/52-2026-09-09-backend-deploy.md#main-stops-being-a-place-you-can-commit-to-2026-09-10-repo-governance--docs-no-code-change) — *"把 funny 项目的 main 分支保护，每日分支，开 PR 的规则借鉴过来"*. The rule here had been the opposite one — 结束任务 said *merge onto `main` and commit there* — which is why this whole log is a straight line of commits on the branch that deploys, and why CI was structurally a **post-mortem**: `check.yml` fires on `pull_request` and on pushes to `main`, and with no PRs only the second half was ever reached, so the four gates reported on a commit `client-deploy`/`server-deploy` had already shipped. Ported from `funny` verbatim: a ruleset named **"Only PR"** (no deletion, no force-push, PR required at **0** approvals — the PR exists for CI and the diff, not a reviewer this repo does not have — and the four jobs required and *strict*), integration on a **daily branch `DD.MM.YYYY`** that task branches merge into `--no-ff` and that opens the day's single PR, and the shared checkout pinned to that branch instead of to `main` — which also retires the hazard that a stray commit in the wrong checkout landed on the thing that ships.
