The admin bypass is copied deliberately too: a single-owner repo that can lock its owner out is the worse failure, so it stays a fire escape. `docs` `platform`
- **09-10** [The lobby, and the login that was still in flight when the menu went live](roadmap/52-2026-09-09-backend-deploy.md#the-lobby-and-the-login-that-was-still-in-flight-when-the-menu-went-live-2026-09-10-client--docs-no-engine-change) — *"这个页面是否需要重新设计 … 然后进入到大厅，大厅里才是关卡，匹配，组队之类的"*, against a screenshot with SQUAD and LOGIN circled. The title screen was doing three jobs, and *"play with other people" had two doors that were never adjacent* — SQUAD on the title screen, CO-OP and PVP one screen deeper. `ModeSelect` is merged into the menu (four screens to a run became three; the phase, the return-phase union and an analytics id all got smaller), and the report's other two asks split: the **auto-login loading** shipped as `platform/identityGate.ts`, the **login page** is refused in a new design/16 section — logging in has never been required to play, a portal forbids a login CTA outright, and the set of hosts that auto-login turns out to have exactly one member, because there is no `wx.login` anywhere in this client. Building the loading state surfaced the bug under it: the silent login landed after the menu was interactive, and on a one-click host the first click starts a RUN, so `syncMetaWithSession` could write the account's server meta over a loadout `beginRun` had already spent. Gate plus guard (`isHubPhase`), because the gate only makes it unlikely. The layout half is the part worth reading twice: a half-width CO-OP/PVP row overflowed its labels in **seven of eight locales** while an eight-locale viewport sweep stayed green — it asserts nothing is off SCREEN, and a label spilling into the next button is not — and closing that hole with an icon-installed label sweep immediately found two overflows older than this pass (German/Italian SETTINGS) and one it would have shipped (`Button.redraw()` re-centring an icon button's label onto its own chip). **Follow-up the same day**: that sweep pointed at every screen found **17 overflowing labels across six**, most of them years older in spirit than this pass — design/17's 2026-08-14 fix had said in writing that only Settings opted in, and nothing had checked the rest since. `client` `ui` `i18n` `docs` `test`

**[2026-09-10 — the bank button that was really a save button](roadmap/53-2026-09-10-boss-only-extraction.md)**

- **09-10** [The bank button that was really a save button](roadmap/53-2026-09-10-boss-only-extraction.md#the-bank-button-that-was-really-a-save-button-2026-09-10-engine--client--docs-engine_version-6061) — *"把中间层的撤离功能去掉，只有打完boss之后的撤离才能退出。对于单人地图，如果没有打完，可以保存进度，下次继续。其实单纯保存指令和随机种子就行了。重进的时候要比对引擎版本"*. Two asks, and the second is why the first is not a nerf: design/05's push-your-luck pillar rested on the per-floor extract/descend choice, and **that choice was doing two jobs** — a player who wanted to stop playing for the evening had to spend the run's whole carry-out decision to do it, because a pause-menu quit forfeits everything. So the early bank was also the save button, and much the worse of the two. Split: the run's only exit is the boss, and an unfinished single-player run is SAVED and resumed. The engine half is four lines and a replay break (`ExtractionSystem` stops consulting `confirmExtract` off the last floor; an ignored press is **dropped, not reinterpreted as a descend**, which would spend a floor card nobody chose) — and **the measurement before the bump is the interesting part: zero golden scenarios diverged**, because none presses `CONFIRM_EXTRACT` and `ember-dungeon-floor1` *cannot* reach a checkpoint at all, which its own note already said. Recorded as a gap rather than read as reassurance; pinned instead by `extraction.test.ts` in BOTH directions, since only the pair pins it. The save format is the report's own (*"单纯保存指令和随机种子"*) and that is exactly right for a stated reason: it is design/08's replay property, so **a save cannot desync from the sim because there is no second description of the sim to disagree with** — a snapshot would have been that second description, going stale on every version that added a field, silently, since a missing field reads as a default. Two invalidations, both refusals: `ENGINE_VERSION` (the ask's own *"比对引擎版本"*), and **content drift, the half `ENGINE_VERSION` does not cover** — `replayFile.ts` embeds the whole config to dodge it, a `localStorage` save cannot afford to, so the config is rebuilt from today's content and its content half fingerprinted, making an edited floor a refusal instead of a player inside a wall. Three things the fast-forward had to get right, each a bug otherwise: the last replayed tick's events are still in `state.events` and `GameLoop` drains what it finds, replaying a burst of flashes and sounds at nobody; a dungeon's geometry is normally built by tick 1's `room_enter`, which the fast-forward consumes; and the score is render-side state the sim cannot reconstruct, so it is carried and restored AFTER the reset that zeroes it. The stream is stored as 7-tuples on a real budget — ~110 bytes keyed × 30 Hz is ~2 MB for ten minutes against ~5 MB for the whole origin, which the account save shares — and a quota failure ABORTS the quit rather than walking someone to the Forge on a promise that dies with the tab. Scope is single-player offline dungeon only, each exclusion a rule (an online run's stream is the server's; a co-op second seat is generated at submit time and is not in the stream); one slot, dropped by **three** call sites, the third being `RunOutcome.handle`, which cannot be folded into the others because nothing routes a victory through `RunLifecycle` — without it, closing the tab on a result screen leaves CONTINUE offered for a run already won and banked. `PortalPrompt` becomes one button (Descend, or Extract on the boss floor) and its label switches to counting the WHOLE carry-out, since that press is now the only exit and the bag is at risk right up to it; the pause menu grows SAVE & QUIT with plain QUIT kept **beside** it, a full row apart, because one button whose meaning depends on the mode is how a run gets lost. Two boundaries respected rather than routed around: `ScreenNav` is in the pure layer and needs the savability RULE, so the module split the way `replayDownload.ts` splits from `MatchRecorder` (`runSave.ts` pure, `runSaveStore.ts` host); and the slot is cached because the Forge asks on every keystroke, which makes the cache the place a stale yes would survive a clear — where most of `runSaveStore.test.ts` lives. `engine` `client` `ui` `i18n` `test` `docs`

**[2026-09-11 — the clock was the whole supply](roadmap/54-2026-09-11-ammo-regen-line.md)**

- **09-11** [The clock was the whole supply](roadmap/54-2026-09-11-ammo-regen-line.md#the-clock-was-the-whole-supply-2026-09-11-engine--client--docs-engine_version-6162) — *"现在的子弹自动回复速度太快了，地图上掉落的子弹价值变得非常低。"* One constant: `ENERGY_REGEN_PER_SEC` 20 → 15, with no price in `content/weaponSpecs/` touched, so the whole roster re-classifies at once — the starter `blaster` lands exactly ON the break-even line and `repeater` crosses above it. **Measured first, and the report was understating it: 98% of every shot fired came off the clock**, at every point of the roster — a `novaburst` loadout funded itself out of regen as thoroughly as a fresh save did. **What that ratio is made of decides what can move it**: `collected x ENERGY_PICKUP_AMOUNT` over a floor's spend, and neither term moves with the regen rate — a floor drops ~7 refills (~210 energy) against ~2000 energy of pulls, so ~10% is the ceiling the DROP TABLE sets even at perfect collection, and the sim still reads 1-2% afterwards. Lowering regen does not raise the floor's SHARE of supply; it changes whether the shortfall is felt at all, and `ENERGY_PICKUP_AMOUNT` stayed 30 because at 2% of supply the drop's size was never the binding term. At 20/s the clock covered a continuously-firing starter outright; at 15/s it covers the unbuffed starter and nothing else, so a `rof_up` stack, a burst and the first interesting gun the floor hands you all run a deficit only the pool and the floor can pay. **22.6% of live ticks now hold a gun the pool cannot pay for (0.9% before), with average floor reached UNMOVED at 0.75.** 10/s was measured and rejected — same dry share, but average floor fell to 0.50, which is re-tuning the level rather than pacing the player; and halving regen while halving the starter's price to keep it sustainable reproduced the bug exactly, because **keeping the starter free and fixing this are the same decision**. The instrument had to be built first: the shipped sim's bot never swaps weapons, so 100% of its pulls were the one gun with no ammo economy at all. `reportFire.ts` grew `spend`, `clock%`/`floor%` and `refills(taken/spawned)` — the pair kept SEPARATE on purpose, and **the first thing it did was catch a claim this entry originally made**: the uncollected refills were blamed on the usefulness gate, with no control, when `material` (no gate at all) reads 21.2% collected against energy's 12.0% over the same runs — the bot misses ~80% of EVERYTHING, so most of that gap is pathing, and at a dozen events over 8 seeds the collection rate cannot A/B this change at all. Golden read before the bump: 2 of 6 scenarios, the other four blind by construction (a full bar makes regen a no-op) — and **both divergences were hash-only, every witness field identical**, which is a red gate nobody can read, so `Witness.energyTotal` now sits beside `hpTotal`, with an anti-vacuity assertion and a measured mutation kill. **Writing the tests is what caught the entry's own wrong answer, and one of the tests it caught was an existing one**: `"the starter blaster outruns its own drain"` ended on `energy === BASE_MAX_ENERGY`, which passes identically at both lines — the last tick of a sawtooth cannot tell climbing from flat from decaying — so its name had stopped being true with nothing red. It asserts the SHAPE now (trough of the last third = trough of the first), beside three new ones: a `rof_up` stack crossing the line (also the seconds-vs-TICKS trap — `buffedCooldown` rounds 6 ticks to 4, not 4.29, so a second `rof_up` buys nothing here), `repeater` paced rather than free, and a gate that break-even survives the conversion to ticks at all. Five go red on the old constants; the rewritten starter test deliberately does not, because a fresh save being near-identical either side IS the safety argument. `engine` `test` `tools` `docs`

- 09-09 [One store for both halves — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"在后端的container里，集成grafana...并用于收集和展示前端的日志"*. design/19 §8 said not to do this, and the row was right when written — what changed is the premise, not the argument: there is a live backend and a public client on three targets now, so there is a sink worth having. **The finding that reframed it came before a line was written**: the borrowed box already runs its owner's full Grafana/Loki/Promtail/Prometheus stack, their promtail scrapes the Docker socket UNFILTERED, so every line our containers ever wrote was already in their store — and it still holds `blightbloom-*` streams from a short-lived container naming on 09-07, i.e. the game's name reached that shared box despite the naming policy, and log CONTENT carries it regardless of the container name. That set the constraint that mattered most: **every service name here is `obs-`prefixed**, because this project joins the host's shared `docker_default` and compose publishes each service NAME as a network alias on it — a service called `loki` would put two containers behind one DNS name, their collector's pushes landing in our store or ours in theirs, intermittently, with nothing failing anywhere. Shipped: four off-the-shelf containers (Loki 14-day, Alloy socket-read-only and filtered to `wnet-test-*`, Prometheus, Grafana at `/grafana/` behind its own login), `src/log.ts` (`HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL [tag] msg k=v` — human-readable because `docker compose logs` is still what anybody reaches for first, and a formatter that guarantees ONE line and ONE logfmt token per value so call sites cannot break the collector's parse), a `/metrics` route per service, and `POST /client/log`. Five decisions each with a way they would have gone wrong: labels are a fixed tiny set with everything identifying in the LINE (a label per session id is one stream per player per visit); telemetry may never affect a player (every outcome is `200 {ok, accepted}` — a 4xx teaches a client to retry forever — and the push is `void`-ed, tested against a deliberately slow stub); **a device clock is not a clock**, so a client timestamp becomes an AGE bounded against the SERVER's clock (a wrong clock otherwise drops the batch AT the store with a 400 nobody here is watching for), in `BigInt` nanoseconds because `ms * 1e6` goes exponential past 1e21 and Loki rejects it; and the account id is resolved from the bearer token server-side, never read from the body. From funny, verbatim and highest-value: `fetch(keepalive, credentials: 'omit')` and **never** `sendBeacon`, which always sends credentialed and so cannot be combined with our `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — get it wrong and the exit flush, the only chance to hear about a crash, silently never lands. The client's ~100 existing `console.error`/`warn` sites are captured by WRAPPING the two methods rather than rewriting them: a missed call site is a class of error that never reports and nothing goes red. A 5-minute `info` heartbeat, because an idle log store and a broken one draw the same empty dashboard. Two deploy-shaped catches: matchsvc's `/metrics` would have been PUBLIC (it is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — refused now by the presence of `x-forwarded-for`, with a 404 rather than a 403), and a missing `BB_GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` stops the whole deploy on purpose (no fallback is available when the image default is admin/admin on a public login page). New gate `deploy.observability.test.ts` runs **Alloy's regex against a line the real logger produces** — six config files referencing each other through text no compiler compares — which is also how the dashboards' "All" level turned out to need `.*` not `.+` (an unparsed line has no `level` label, and an absent label matches only the empty string). `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
- 09-09 [Retention, and the sentence a policy stopped being able to say](roadmap/48-2026-09-09-analytics.md#retention-and-the-sentence-a-policy-stopped-being-able-to-say-2026-09-09-client--server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"funny的后台管理系统，我们这边应该也需要，只是不需要那么多功能。主要的用户管理和留存打点肯定是要的"*, then *"隐私政策改说明。暴露到公网。先文档再按顺序实现"*. The ask was `funny`'s ops backend (5,285 + 6,756 lines); almost none of it is here, and **why not is worth more than what is**. Two things already in place shrank it to a third: volume 47 landed the log store the same morning, so the monitoring half was answered — and **Grafana IS an ops frontend**, already deployed behind its own login, which is why `funny` hand-wrote 6,756 lines of one and this does not. That fixed the scoping rule for [design/21-ops-analytics.md](21-ops-analytics.md): anything expressible as a time series goes to Prometheus and is drawn by Grafana; a bespoke page is justified only for what a time series cannot express, and **exactly one thing qualifies — a row per player**. The owner asked for the console on the public internet, which raises the bar rather than lowering it, and the answer is the decision the rest hangs off: **it is read-only over player data, and every player-data mutation stays a CLI script on the box**, where SSH access is already the second factor. That single choice deletes RBAC, the four-role matrix, the compensation approval workflow and the audit-visibility split — the largest block of `funny`'s admin — because there are no writes to gate; and it supersedes design/19 §7's *"No admin service… revisit when the first refund arrives"* by a different trigger than the one it named. **Phase A shipped**: one shared vocabulary imported by both sides through `@dd/net/*` (two copies that drift produce no type error and no red test, only an event sent forever and dropped forever), `POST /client/events` as a sibling of `/client/log` sharing its limiter and its always-200 contract, a third SQLite database, an hourly rollup whose gauges land on the existing `/metrics`, a Grafana dashboard, and the client SDK. Four load-bearing decisions: `daily_active(day, install)` is its own table so retention does not DEPEND on the prune (otherwise the day the events window drops a week, D7 for that week silently becomes `0` rather than unknown) and so DAU-by-host is a PARTITION that sums to DAU; the rollup **writes before it exposes**, because Prometheus keeps 15 days and a gauge cannot be backfilled; **an unaged cohort is ABSENT, never zero** — a gauge has no "unknown", and in a launch week every offset is unaged, where a 0 would read as *nobody ever came back*; and collection is opt-in by the same env var the backup worker discovers the file by, so "collected" and "backed up" cannot disagree. **The install id turned out to already exist** — `daydayup.playerId.v1`, stored since the PvP squad work — so analytics adds no new stored identifier and retention is continuous from day one; what it could not reuse is `getPlayerId()`, which prefers the account id and would change a player's identity mid-cohort when they log in. Three events are **derived from the frame** rather than announced from the fifteen places that write `RunState.phase`, which also makes **abandonment detected rather than reported** — so a quit route nobody thought of is still counted. Two vocabulary entries came back OUT for the same reason (a declared field nothing can populate is an always-empty panel): `run_start.weapon`, because no authored weapon id survives into the sim state, and `store_open`, because opening the store IS a `screen_view`. **The privacy policy could no longer say what it said** — and the bullet was not the problem, the callout was: *"Playing without an account collects nothing on our server"* is exactly false for analytics, since a guest is who retention is about. Found while rewriting it: **the localStorage list was already wrong**, naming three items while the shipped game has written a fourth all along. Every number in it is now a number the code deletes on (90 days / 180 / 14, the last checked against the log store's own config rather than asserted), and the cohort window came down from 400 days to 180 because a policy has to state the number and the number has to be defensible. **One host is deliberately excluded and that is the interesting part**: WeChat has no `localStorage`, so the install id would be minted per visit — retention would read 0% instead of absent, but **DAU would report VISITS while labelled distinct installs**, which is worse, because it is a plausible number nobody would question. No data beats wrong data. **Running it found two defects neither the tests nor the review did.** Every abandoned run reported no floor and no duration — quitting tears the engine down, so the props were read from a state already `null`, and the one number the event exists for was missing from every row; no test could have caught it, because at that seam the state is an argument. And **every flush paid its own CORS preflight, including the exit one** (no `access-control-max-age` on the shared block) — the one request that cannot be retried, since `keepalive` protects a request already STARTED and an uncached preflight makes the `pagehide` flush two round trips behind an unloading document, i.e. exactly the `session_end` funny lost outright. Both fixed and verified in live traffic. New gate `deploy.dashboardMetrics.test.ts`: every metric a panel queries must be one the server emits, because a dashboard is the one artefact whose broken state looks exactly like its working state — proven the same day by volume 47's own fully-populated, meaningless panel. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
- 09-09 [A console that cannot write, and a flag that does nothing](roadmap/49-2026-09-09-ops-console.md#a-console-that-cannot-write-and-a-flag-that-does-nothing-2026-09-09-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"继续"*, against design/21 §7's own order. Phase B is a FIFTH process (`server/src/adminsvc/`, 8790) rather than routes on matchsvc, because matchsvc is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — a route added there is public the moment it exists, which is why `/metrics` had to explicitly refuse proxied requests, and a panel built there would make every future admin route inherit *public unless it remembers not to be*. The property that makes a publicly-exposed console proportionate is the FORM of decision B1: **it is not that the console does not write player data, it is that it cannot** — `readOnly: true` handles on all three databases, plus `:ro` bind mounts in compose, so it is refused once by SQLite and once by the kernel. Asserted the only way that claim can be: a test ATTEMPTS an INSERT, an UPDATE, a DELETE and a DROP through each handle and requires each to throw, with a `SELECT` beside them as the control (checking the option was passed would pass against a handle that ignored it). **Every handle is nullable and none of the nulls is defensive**: `readOnly` does not create a missing file, and all three belong to other processes — so the console comes up with per-section "unavailable" cards carrying the reason, proven by booting the real bundle with none of the three present, because a process that refused to start could not be used to find out why the file is not there. **The design named the wrong table for one column**: §3.2 said last-active comes from `daily_active`, which holds no account id at all — by design, since a cohort is a question about a browser and joining it to an account would assemble exactly the profile §2.1 promises not to. It comes from `events.account_id`, which is pruned at 90 days, so a blank cell means *no event in the window* and never *never played* — and the page says so, because a column that conflated those would be read as the second one. The retention grid carries volume 48's rule one layer out: an unaged cohort arrives as a MISSING ROW, so `CohortCell` is `{rate,size} | null` and the fixture is built by running the shipped `persistRollup` once per simulated day, giving a measured 50%, a measured 0% and an unknown side by side; the page prints the rollup row count too, since an empty grid over zero rows is an empty database and over three hundred is a bug in the reader. **The XSS this console is really exposed to is `webhook_events.raw`** — bytes an outsider POSTed to the billing plane, rendered in the browser of the one account that can read every player's row — so the view modules deliberately do NOT pre-escape (no "already safe" category to reason about), `esc` is the single gate, and the test drives the whole chain from the real billing schema through the real read-only handle to the rendered page. A test also caught a live bug: the cohort tooltip read "4 of 4 installs returned" on a 50% cell, because `daily_rollup` stores the rate and the size and **not** the return count — a tooltip that contradicts its own percentage is the kind of instrument that gets believed. **Phase C's finding is a switch that does nothing.** Two of the four flags are about the CLIENT, and §4's delivery mechanism is an `x-internal-key` endpoint a browser cannot call and must never be able to — so they have a row, a control, and nothing on the other end. This project has paid for that shape once (volume 47's fully-populated, meaningless panel), so the state is in the TYPE, rendered as a `not delivered` badge, and pinned by a test so it cannot be "fixed" by flipping the boolean instead of building the path. **A flag captured at construction is not a flag**: `Matchmaker` held its two timings as numbers, so a console change would only land on the next restart; they are suppliers now, read per decision. C1 is enforced on BOTH sides of the table (`setFlag` will not write an unknown name, `readOverrides` will not return one — the two are edited by different people at different times), the poll parse is all-or-nothing (a partial merge lets a garbled response revert a deliberate override because a *different* flag was corrupt), and the allowlist test pins the exact set of names on purpose: it is the mechanism, not a check. Every new module is at **100% lines and branches** except the entry point's `require.main` guard, and getting there DELETED five branches rather than testing them — a branch no input can reach is one a coverage gate cannot tell from an untested one, which is the shape that gets removed in a refactor with nothing going red. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

- 09-09 [One store for both halves — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"在后端的container里，集成grafana...并用于收集和展示前端的日志"*. design/19 §8 said not to do this, and the row was right when written — what changed is the premise, not the argument: there is a live backend and a public client on three targets now, so there is a sink worth having. **The finding that reframed it came before a line was written**: the borrowed box already runs its owner's full Grafana/Loki/Promtail/Prometheus stack, their promtail scrapes the Docker socket UNFILTERED, so every line our containers ever wrote was already in their store — and it still holds `blightbloom-*` streams from a short-lived container naming on 09-07, i.e. the game's name reached that shared box despite the naming policy, and log CONTENT carries it regardless of the container name. That set the constraint that mattered most: **every service name here is `obs-`prefixed**, because this project joins the host's shared `docker_default` and compose publishes each service NAME as a network alias on it — a service called `loki` would put two containers behind one DNS name, their collector's pushes landing in our store or ours in theirs, intermittently, with nothing failing anywhere. Shipped: four off-the-shelf containers (Loki 14-day, Alloy socket-read-only and filtered to `wnet-test-*`, Prometheus, Grafana at `/grafana/` behind its own login), `src/log.ts` (`HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL [tag] msg k=v` — human-readable because `docker compose logs` is still what anybody reaches for first, and a formatter that guarantees ONE line and ONE logfmt token per value so call sites cannot break the collector's parse), a `/metrics` route per service, and `POST /client/log`. Five decisions each with a way they would have gone wrong: labels are a fixed tiny set with everything identifying in the LINE (a label per session id is one stream per player per visit); telemetry may never affect a player (every outcome is `200 {ok, accepted}` — a 4xx teaches a client to retry forever — and the push is `void`-ed, tested against a deliberately slow stub); **a device clock is not a clock**, so a client timestamp becomes an AGE bounded against the SERVER's clock (a wrong clock otherwise drops the batch AT the store with a 400 nobody here is watching for), in `BigInt` nanoseconds because `ms * 1e6` goes exponential past 1e21 and Loki rejects it; and the account id is resolved from the bearer token server-side, never read from the body. From funny, verbatim and highest-value: `fetch(keepalive, credentials: 'omit')` and **never** `sendBeacon`, which always sends credentialed and so cannot be combined with our `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — get it wrong and the exit flush, the only chance to hear about a crash, silently never lands. The client's ~100 existing `console.error`/`warn` sites are captured by WRAPPING the two methods rather than rewriting them: a missed call site is a class of error that never reports and nothing goes red. A 5-minute `info` heartbeat, because an idle log store and a broken one draw the same empty dashboard. Two deploy-shaped catches: matchsvc's `/metrics` would have been PUBLIC (it is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — refused now by the presence of `x-forwarded-for`, with a 404 rather than a 403), and a missing `BB_GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` stops the whole deploy on purpose (no fallback is available when the image default is admin/admin on a public login page). New gate `deploy.observability.test.ts` runs **Alloy's regex against a line the real logger produces** — six config files referencing each other through text no compiler compares — which is also how the dashboards' "All" level turned out to need `.*` not `.+` (an unparsed line has no `level` label, and an absent label matches only the empty string). `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
- 09-09 [Retention, and the sentence a policy stopped being able to say](roadmap/48-2026-09-09-analytics.md#retention-and-the-sentence-a-policy-stopped-being-able-to-say-2026-09-09-client--server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"funny的后台管理系统，我们这边应该也需要，只是不需要那么多功能。主要的用户管理和留存打点肯定是要的"*, then *"隐私政策改说明。暴露到公网。先文档再按顺序实现"*. The ask was `funny`'s ops backend (5,285 + 6,756 lines); almost none of it is here, and **why not is worth more than what is**. Two things already in place shrank it to a third: volume 47 landed the log store the same morning, so the monitoring half was answered — and **Grafana IS an ops frontend**, already deployed behind its own login, which is why `funny` hand-wrote 6,756 lines of one and this does not. That fixed the scoping rule for [design/21-ops-analytics.md](21-ops-analytics.md): anything expressible as a time series goes to Prometheus and is drawn by Grafana; a bespoke page is justified only for what a time series cannot express, and **exactly one thing qualifies — a row per player**. The owner asked for the console on the public internet, which raises the bar rather than lowering it, and the answer is the decision the rest hangs off: **it is read-only over player data, and every player-data mutation stays a CLI script on the box**, where SSH access is already the second factor. That single choice deletes RBAC, the four-role matrix, the compensation approval workflow and the audit-visibility split — the largest block of `funny`'s admin — because there are no writes to gate; and it supersedes design/19 §7's *"No admin service… revisit when the first refund arrives"* by a different trigger than the one it named. **Phase A shipped**: one shared vocabulary imported by both sides through `@dd/net/*` (two copies that drift produce no type error and no red test, only an event sent forever and dropped forever), `POST /client/events` as a sibling of `/client/log` sharing its limiter and its always-200 contract, a third SQLite database, an hourly rollup whose gauges land on the existing `/metrics`, a Grafana dashboard, and the client SDK. Four load-bearing decisions: `daily_active(day, install)` is its own table so retention does not DEPEND on the prune (otherwise the day the events window drops a week, D7 for that week silently becomes `0` rather than unknown) and so DAU-by-host is a PARTITION that sums to DAU; the rollup **writes before it exposes**, because Prometheus keeps 15 days and a gauge cannot be backfilled; **an unaged cohort is ABSENT, never zero** — a gauge has no "unknown", and in a launch week every offset is unaged, where a 0 would read as *nobody ever came back*; and collection is opt-in by the same env var the backup worker discovers the file by, so "collected" and "backed up" cannot disagree. **The install id turned out to already exist** — `daydayup.playerId.v1`, stored since the PvP squad work — so analytics adds no new stored identifier and retention is continuous from day one; what it could not reuse is `getPlayerId()`, which prefers the account id and would change a player's identity mid-cohort when they log in. Three events are **derived from the frame** rather than announced from the fifteen places that write `RunState.phase`, which also makes **abandonment detected rather than reported** — so a quit route nobody thought of is still counted. Two vocabulary entries came back OUT for the same reason (a declared field nothing can populate is an always-empty panel): `run_start.weapon`, because no authored weapon id survives into the sim state, and `store_open`, because opening the store IS a `screen_view`. **The privacy policy could no longer say what it said** — and the bullet was not the problem, the callout was: *"Playing without an account collects nothing on our server"* is exactly false for analytics, since a guest is who retention is about. Found while rewriting it: **the localStorage list was already wrong**, naming three items while the shipped game has written a fourth all along. Every number in it is now a number the code deletes on (90 days / 180 / 14, the last checked against the log store's own config rather than asserted), and the cohort window came down from 400 days to 180 because a policy has to state the number and the number has to be defensible. **One host is deliberately excluded and that is the interesting part**: WeChat has no `localStorage`, so the install id would be minted per visit — retention would read 0% instead of absent, but **DAU would report VISITS while labelled distinct installs**, which is worse, because it is a plausible number nobody would question. No data beats wrong data. **Running it found two defects neither the tests nor the review did.** Every abandoned run reported no floor and no duration — quitting tears the engine down, so the props were read from a state already `null`, and the one number the event exists for was missing from every row; no test could have caught it, because at that seam the state is an argument. And **every flush paid its own CORS preflight, including the exit one** (no `access-control-max-age` on the shared block) — the one request that cannot be retried, since `keepalive` protects a request already STARTED and an uncached preflight makes the `pagehide` flush two round trips behind an unloading document, i.e. exactly the `session_end` funny lost outright. Both fixed and verified in live traffic. New gate `deploy.dashboardMetrics.test.ts`: every metric a panel queries must be one the server emits, because a dashboard is the one artefact whose broken state looks exactly like its working state — proven the same day by volume 47's own fully-populated, meaningless panel. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
- 09-09 [A console that cannot write, and a flag that does nothing](roadmap/49-2026-09-09-ops-console.md#a-console-that-cannot-write-and-a-flag-that-does-nothing-2026-09-09-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"继续"*, against design/21 §7's own order. Phase B is a FIFTH process (`server/src/adminsvc/`, 8790) rather than routes on matchsvc, because matchsvc is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — a route added there is public the moment it exists, which is why `/metrics` had to explicitly refuse proxied requests, and a panel built there would make every future admin route inherit *public unless it remembers not to be*. The property that makes a publicly-exposed console proportionate is the FORM of decision B1: **it is not that the console does not write player data, it is that it cannot** — `readOnly: true` handles on all three databases, plus `:ro` bind mounts in compose, so it is refused once by SQLite and once by the kernel. Asserted the only way that claim can be: a test ATTEMPTS an INSERT, an UPDATE, a DELETE and a DROP through each handle and requires each to throw, with a `SELECT` beside them as the control (checking the option was passed would pass against a handle that ignored it). **Every handle is nullable and none of the nulls is defensive**: `readOnly` does not create a missing file, and all three belong to other processes — so the console comes up with per-section "unavailable" cards carrying the reason, proven by booting the real bundle with none of the three present, because a process that refused to start could not be used to find out why the file is not there. **The design named the wrong table for one column**: §3.2 said last-active comes from `daily_active`, which holds no account id at all — by design, since a cohort is a question about a browser and joining it to an account would assemble exactly the profile §2.1 promises not to. It comes from `events.account_id`, which is pruned at 90 days, so a blank cell means *no event in the window* and never *never played* — and the page says so, because a column that conflated those would be read as the second one. The retention grid carries volume 48's rule one layer out: an unaged cohort arrives as a MISSING ROW, so `CohortCell` is `{rate,size} | null` and the fixture is built by running the shipped `persistRollup` once per simulated day, giving a measured 50%, a measured 0% and an unknown side by side; the page prints the rollup row count too, since an empty grid over zero rows is an empty database and over three hundred is a bug in the reader. **The XSS this console is really exposed to is `webhook_events.raw`** — bytes an outsider POSTed to the billing plane, rendered in the browser of the one account that can read every player's row — so the view modules deliberately do NOT pre-escape (no "already safe" category to reason about), `esc` is the single gate, and the test drives the whole chain from the real billing schema through the real read-only handle to the rendered page. A test also caught a live bug: the cohort tooltip read "4 of 4 installs returned" on a 50% cell, because `daily_rollup` stores the rate and the size and **not** the return count — a tooltip that contradicts its own percentage is the kind of instrument that gets believed. **Phase C's finding is a switch that does nothing.** Two of the four flags are about the CLIENT, and §4's delivery mechanism is an `x-internal-key` endpoint a browser cannot call and must never be able to — so they have a row, a control, and nothing on the other end. This project has paid for that shape once (volume 47's fully-populated, meaningless panel), so the state is in the TYPE, rendered as a `not delivered` badge, and pinned by a test so it cannot be "fixed" by flipping the boolean instead of building the path. **A flag captured at construction is not a flag**: `Matchmaker` held its two timings as numbers, so a console change would only land on the next restart; they are suppliers now, read per decision. C1 is enforced on BOTH sides of the table (`setFlag` will not write an unknown name, `readOverrides` will not return one — the two are edited by different people at different times), the poll parse is all-or-nothing (a partial merge lets a garbled response revert a deliberate override because a *different* flag was corrupt), and the allowlist test pins the exact set of names on purpose: it is the mechanism, not a check. Every new module is at **100% lines and branches** except the entry point's `require.main` guard, and getting there DELETED five branches rather than testing them — a branch no input can reach is one a coverage gate cannot tell from an untested one, which is the shape that gets removed in a refactor with nothing going red. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

- 09-09 [One store for both halves — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — the backend's logs and the browser's](roadmap/47-2026-09-09-observability.md#one-store-for-both-halves-the-backends-logs-and-the-browsers-2026-09-09-server--client--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"在后端的container里，集成grafana...并用于收集和展示前端的日志"*. design/19 §8 said not to do this, and the row was right when written — what changed is the premise, not the argument: there is a live backend and a public client on three targets now, so there is a sink worth having. **The finding that reframed it came before a line was written**: the borrowed box already runs its owner's full Grafana/Loki/Promtail/Prometheus stack, their promtail scrapes the Docker socket UNFILTERED, so every line our containers ever wrote was already in their store — and it still holds `blightbloom-*` streams from a short-lived container naming on 09-07, i.e. the game's name reached that shared box despite the naming policy, and log CONTENT carries it regardless of the container name. That set the constraint that mattered most: **every service name here is `obs-`prefixed**, because this project joins the host's shared `docker_default` and compose publishes each service NAME as a network alias on it — a service called `loki` would put two containers behind one DNS name, their collector's pushes landing in our store or ours in theirs, intermittently, with nothing failing anywhere. Shipped: four off-the-shelf containers (Loki 14-day, Alloy socket-read-only and filtered to `wnet-test-*`, Prometheus, Grafana at `/grafana/` behind its own login), `src/log.ts` (`HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL [tag] msg k=v` — human-readable because `docker compose logs` is still what anybody reaches for first, and a formatter that guarantees ONE line and ONE logfmt token per value so call sites cannot break the collector's parse), a `/metrics` route per service, and `POST /client/log`. Five decisions each with a way they would have gone wrong: labels are a fixed tiny set with everything identifying in the LINE (a label per session id is one stream per player per visit); telemetry may never affect a player (every outcome is `200 {ok, accepted}` — a 4xx teaches a client to retry forever — and the push is `void`-ed, tested against a deliberately slow stub); **a device clock is not a clock**, so a client timestamp becomes an AGE bounded against the SERVER's clock (a wrong clock otherwise drops the batch AT the store with a 400 nobody here is watching for), in `BigInt` nanoseconds because `ms * 1e6` goes exponential past 1e21 and Loki rejects it; and the account id is resolved from the bearer token server-side, never read from the body. From funny, verbatim and highest-value: `fetch(keepalive, credentials: 'omit')` and **never** `sendBeacon`, which always sends credentialed and so cannot be combined with our `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — get it wrong and the exit flush, the only chance to hear about a crash, silently never lands. The client's ~100 existing `console.error`/`warn` sites are captured by WRAPPING the two methods rather than rewriting them: a missed call site is a class of error that never reports and nothing goes red. A 5-minute `info` heartbeat, because an idle log store and a broken one draw the same empty dashboard. Two deploy-shaped catches: matchsvc's `/metrics` would have been PUBLIC (it is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — refused now by the presence of `x-forwarded-for`, with a 404 rather than a 403), and a missing `BB_GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` stops the whole deploy on purpose (no fallback is available when the image default is admin/admin on a public login page). New gate `deploy.observability.test.ts` runs **Alloy's regex against a line the real logger produces** — six config files referencing each other through text no compiler compares — which is also how the dashboards' "All" level turned out to need `.*` not `.+` (an unparsed line has no `level` label, and an absent label matches only the empty string). `platform` `net` `test` `docs`

And **every flush paid its own CORS preflight, including the exit one** (no `access-control-max-age` on the shared block) — the one request that cannot be retried, since `keepalive` protects a request already STARTED and an uncached preflight makes the `pagehide` flush two round trips behind an unloading document, i.e. exactly the `session_end` funny lost outright. Both fixed and verified in live traffic. New gate `deploy.dashboardMetrics.test.ts`: every metric a panel queries must be one the server emits, because a dashboard is the one artefact whose broken state looks exactly like its working state — proven the same day by volume 47's own fully-populated, meaningless panel. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
- 09-09 [A console that cannot write, and a flag that does nothing](roadmap/49-2026-09-09-ops-console.md#a-console-that-cannot-write-and-a-flag-that-does-nothing-2026-09-09-server--deploy--docs-no-engine-change) — *"继续"*, against design/21 §7's own order. Phase B is a FIFTH process (`server/src/adminsvc/`, 8790) rather than routes on matchsvc, because matchsvc is the one service Caddy proxies wholesale — a route added there is public the moment it exists, which is why `/metrics` had to explicitly refuse proxied requests, and a panel built there would make every future admin route inherit *public unless it remembers not to be*. The property that makes a publicly-exposed console proportionate is the FORM of decision B1: **it is not that the console does not write player data, it is that it cannot** — `readOnly: true` handles on all three databases, plus `:ro` bind mounts in compose, so it is refused once by SQLite and once by the kernel. Asserted the only way that claim can be: a test ATTEMPTS an INSERT, an UPDATE, a DELETE and a DROP through each handle and requires each to throw, with a `SELECT` beside them as the control (checking the option was passed would pass against a handle that ignored it). **Every handle is nullable and none of the nulls is defensive**: `readOnly` does not create a missing file, and all three belong to other processes — so the console comes up with per-section "unavailable" cards carrying the reason, proven by booting the real bundle with none of the three present, because a process that refused to start could not be used to find out why the file is not there. **The design named the wrong table for one column**: §3.2 said last-active comes from `daily_active`, which holds no account id at all — by design, since a cohort is a question about a browser and joining it to an account would assemble exactly the profile §2.1 promises not to. It comes from `events.account_id`, which is pruned at 90 days, so a blank cell means *no event in the window* and never *never played* — and the page says so, because a column that conflated those would be read as the second one. The retention grid carries volume 48's rule one layer out: an unaged cohort arrives as a MISSING ROW, so `CohortCell` is `{rate,size} | null` and the fixture is built by running the shipped `persistRollup` once per simulated day, giving a measured 50%, a measured 0% and an unknown side by side; the page prints the rollup row count too, since an empty grid over zero rows is an empty database and over three hundred is a bug in the reader. **The XSS this console is really exposed to is `webhook_events.raw`** — bytes an outsider POSTed to the billing plane, rendered in the browser of the one account that can read every player's row — so the view modules deliberately do NOT pre-escape (no "already safe" category to reason about), `esc` is the single gate, and the test drives the whole chain from the real billing schema through the real read-only handle to the rendered page. A test also caught a live bug: the cohort tooltip read "4 of 4 installs returned" on a 50% cell, because `daily_rollup` stores the rate and the size and **not** the return count — a tooltip that contradicts its own percentage is the kind of instrument that gets believed. **Phase C's finding is a switch that does nothing.** Two of the four flags are about the CLIENT, and §4's delivery mechanism is an `x-internal-key` endpoint a browser cannot call and must never be able to — so they have a row, a control, and nothing on the other end. This project has paid for that shape once (volume 47's fully-populated, meaningless panel), so the state is in the TYPE, rendered as a `not delivered` badge, and pinned by a test so it cannot be "fixed" by flipping the boolean instead of building the path. **A flag captured at construction is not a flag**: `Matchmaker` held its two timings as numbers, so a console change would only land on the next restart; they are suppliers now, read per decision. C1 is enforced on BOTH sides of the table (`setFlag` will not write an unknown name, `readOverrides` will not return one — the two are edited by different people at different times), the poll parse is all-or-nothing (a partial merge lets a garbled response revert a deliberate override because a *different* flag was corrupt), and the allowlist test pins the exact set of names on purpose: it is the mechanism, not a check. Every new module is at **100% lines and branches** except the entry point's `require.main` guard, and getting there DELETED five branches rather than testing them — a branch no input can reach is one a coverage gate cannot tell from an untested one, which is the shape that gets removed in a refactor with nothing going red. `platform` `net` `test` `docs`
